## Review

**8 out of 10, Strong.** Presentation quality, judged with the SlideDeck Co review heuristic (https://slidedeck.co/review-methodology/). The ask, the evidence and the merit of the idea are not rated.

The deck makes one argument and builds it in order: platform shifts (2-12), then inside tech (14-48), then outside tech (49-72), with section dividers and a consistent system of action titles, subtitles, sources and restrained red-and-grey colour. Charts are mostly clean and direct, such as the capex stack on 17, the Nvidia revenue lines on 20-21 and the model-convergence bars on 35, and the circle-chain diagrams repeat a recognisable logic. Weaknesses are consequential: footer text is very faint, the legends on several charts sit small and far from the data (17), some stacked charts use similar tints that are hard to separate (38, 55), and several slides are bare quotes on a white box (15, 23, 63) or screenshot collages with small unreadable text (7, 29, 31, 70). Some slides also leave an unused empty left column (2, 12), and a few near-duplicate slides (12 against 2, 20 against 21) depend on the speaker to carry the build.

**Worth keeping:** Keep the three-part route, the full-sentence titles with explanatory subtitles, the consistent red highlight for the key series or answer, and the sourced single-message charts.

**Most useful repair:** Enlarge legends, footers and source lines, separate the pale tints in the stacked charts, and crop or annotate screenshot slides so the one relevant headline is readable.

**What would change the score:** I would replace the empty left margin on slides 2 and 12 with usable space, shrink the quote-only slides' white boxes, and add direct labels or takeaways on the busiest multi-series charts.

_Confidence medium. 72 of 90 pages inspected on contact sheets, 4 at readable size. Reviewed Reviewed 2026-11-08. Model-assisted review (claude-sonnet-5-5)._

## Summary

The deck opens with a title slide and frames generative AI as the next platform shift, following earlier shifts from mainframes through PCs, the web and smartphones. It argues that leaders often lose dominance, that early hype and bubbles recur, and that the outcome of a platform shift is hard to predict. The middle sections split the topic into two parts. Inside tech covers large capital spending by Big Tech, data centre build-out, chip supply, circular financing among AI firms, model convergence and user adoption. Outside tech covers how businesses absorb AI for obvious use cases, the role of human oversight, the Jevons paradox, advertising and the question of where value will be captured. The deck closes with historical examples of automation, a quote on AI as whatever machines cannot yet do, a thank-you slide and a short biography of the speaker.

## Questions about this deck

**How many slides does the Benedict Evans deck have?**

The Benedict Evans deck is listed as having 90 pages. Slide 1 is the title slide, and the deck closes with a thank-you slide and a short speaker biography.

**What does the Benedict Evans deck say about Nvidia's cash flow and chip supply?**

Slide 32 states that Nvidia has $77bn of TTM FCF and that TSMC can't keep up with demand. Slide 21 adds that TSMC is unwilling or unable to expand capacity fast enough to meet Nvidia's book.

**What does Benedict Evans say about Big Tech capex in 2025?**

Slide 17 says there is about $400bn of capex in 2025 for the big four alone, and notes that global telecoms is about $300bn for comparison. Slide 18 gives the same comparison with a planned 2025 figure of about $350bn for the big four.

**How is the Benedict Evans deck structured?**

Slide 3 frames the deck around three parts: The New Thing, Inside tech and Outside tech. Slide 14 is the Inside tech divider, and the deck closes with a thank-you slide and a speaker biography.

### Slide 4

**Text**

Dominance is won and lost / Microsoft dominated the PC era, but when the centre of gravity shifted to smartphones it became irrelevant / Microsoft OS share of global computer unit sales / 100% / 75% / 50% / 25% / 0% / 1975 / 1980 / 1985 / 1990 / 1995 / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / 2025 / Source: Apple, Google, Gartner / Benedict Evans / –– November 2025 / 4

**Layout**

Three horizontal bands: a light grey header band at top, a white central band holding the chart, and a light grey footer band at bottom. Title sits top-left at about x 20.5% with the subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned on the same left edge. The chart title sits left-aligned at x 20.6% and the chart plot spans roughly x 19% to 95% of the width, with the y-axis labels to its left and the x-axis labels beneath it. The source line is left-aligned at x 20.5% in the footer; the author, date and slide number 4 are right-aligned at the footer's right end, with the page number at about x 94.9%. Margins are about 20% on the left and about 5% on the right.

**Visuals**

Chart: line chart titled 'Microsoft OS share of global computer unit sales'. Y axis: percent from 0% to 100%, labels at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%, with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled value and a solid grey baseline at 0%. X axis: years 1975 to 2025, labels every five years (1975, 1980, ... 2025), with a thin grey baseline. Single series, a smooth red line about 3 px wide with no markers, starting at 1980 at about 0% and ending at 2025 at about 12%. Values are not printed on the slide; the following are estimates marked about: 1981 about 2%, 1982 about 9%, 1983 about 26%, 1984 about 31%, 1985 about 49%, 1987 about 65%, 1988 about 79%, 1990 about 84%, 1991 about 77%, 1992 about 84%, 1995 about 91%, 1998 about 97%, 2000 about 97%, 2004 about 97%, 2006 about 96%, 2008 about 90%, 2010 about 69%, 2011 about 48%, 2013 about 20%, 2016 about 11%, 2018 about 12%, 2021 about 16%, 2023 about 13%, 2025 about 12%. Legend: none. Highlighted points: none. Line colour red approximately #E8473F. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 across the full slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Flat, minimal look with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners. Background is light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands and white #FFFFFF for the central band. Title colour is dark grey #545454 in a serif (MessinaModern-Regular, a modern serif similar to Century-style book faces) at regular weight, title size about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle is #545454 in the same serif at about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis labels are in a grotesque sans-serif (MessinaSans, Regular and SemiBold) in #545454 at about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height. Footer author name is bold sans in #A6A6A6, date and page number are book-weight sans in #A6A6A6, all about 1.85% of slide height. Accent colour is the red chart line, approximately #E8473F. Gridlines are thin dotted grey lines, the baseline a thin solid grey line.

### Slide 5

5

**Layout**

Full-width header band (top 0 to about 20.5% of height) holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about 20.5% of width. A white chart panel fills the middle band, with a chart legend at the far left (x about 4.8%) and the plot area running from about x 20.5% to 95.2%. A grey footer band (about 86 to 100% height) holds the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right, with the page number at about x 94.9%, y 91.1%.

**Visuals**

One chart: a 100% stacked column chart titled Global PC unit sales share. Columns are annual, one per year from 1975 to 2000, with no gaps between columns but thin white separators. Vertical axis: 0% to 100% share of global PC unit sales, labelled at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75% and 100%. Horizontal axis: years 1975 to 2000, labelled every five years. Series stacked bottom to top: Apple in red (#E74C3C approx.), All others in light pink (#F8DBD8), IBM-compatible in dark grey (#535353). Estimated values, read from the image without printed data labels: 1975 Apple 0%, All others about 100%. 1979 Apple about 9%, All others about 91%. 1981 Apple about 14%, All others about 84%, IBM-compatible about 2%. 1983 Apple about 11%, All others about 54%, IBM-compatible about 35%. 1984 Apple about 29%, All others about 29%, IBM-compatible about 42%. 1985 Apple about 21%, All others about 10%, IBM-compatible about 69%. 1990 Apple about 7%, All others about 7%, IBM-compatible about 86%. 1995 Apple about 9%, All others about 1%, IBM-compatible about 90%. 2000 Apple about 3%, All others about 0%, IBM-compatible about 97%. Legend: a vertical list at top-left of the plot with small square swatches in dark grey, light pink and red, reading IBM-compatible, All others, Apple from top to bottom. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands, white #FFFFFF for the central chart band. Clean corporate editorial style. Header and footer bands are light grey #F1F1F1; the chart panel is white #FFFFFF. Title is a high-contrast serif (MessinaModern-Regular, close to a Clarendon-like slab serif) in regular weight, dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle is the same serif at about 2.6% of slide height, #545454. Chart title and axis labels are a geometric sans (MessinaSans) at about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height, in #545454 or #535353. Source line is MessinaSans-Regular #545454. Author name is MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number are MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6. Data colours: red accent for Apple, pale pink for All others, dark grey #535353 for IBM-compatible. Thin white (#FFFFFF) dividing lines between columns, no shadows, no gradients, no rounded corners. Dash-like double hyphen separates author and date.

### Slide 6

**Text**

How will the new thing work? We don’t know
For every new platform, we forget how many ideas failed and how unclear everything was
Internet
AOL, Yahoo,
Pointcast, Flash,
plugins, portals…
Sun? Netscape?
Mobile internet
i-mode, J2ME, WAP,
mesh, DVB-H,
keyboards…
Nokia? RIM?
Generative AI?
Browsers? Agentic?
Voice? MCP?
Wearables? GEO?
Benedict Evans
–– November 2025
6

**Layout**

Three equal white circles sit in a horizontal row across the middle-lower part of the slide, joined by two small grey triangular arrows pointing right, and the circles are centred between roughly x 19.6% and x 96.2%. The title sits top-left aligned at x 20.5% with a one-line subtitle directly beneath it at x 20.6%, and the footer (author, date, page number) sits at the bottom right at y 91.1%, ending near the right margin at x 95.5%.

**Visuals**

Three circles (ellipses with equal size, about 21.7% wide and 38.5% high each, filled white #FFFFFF). Circle 1 (left) and circle 2 (middle) have a dark grey #545454 outline about 0.3% of slide height thick; circle 3 (right) has a red #E84C3D outline of the same thickness, the only highlighted element. Each circle holds a bold heading and light body lines centred inside it: circle 1 'Internet' with 'AOL, Yahoo, / Pointcast, Flash, / plugins, portals…' and 'Sun? Netscape?'; circle 2 'Mobile internet' with 'i-mode, J2ME, WAP, / mesh, DVB-H, / keyboards…' and 'Nokia? RIM?'; circle 3 'Generative AI?' with 'Browsers? Agentic? / Voice? MCP? / Wearables? GEO?'. Two solid right-pointing isosceles triangles in light grey #A6A6A6, each about 1.9% wide and 6.9% high, sit vertically centred between circles at x about 43.2% and 70.7% (y about 50.0% to 57.0%), acting as sequence arrows from left to right. No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the entire slide. Flat, minimal style on a light grey background with no gradients, shadows or textures. Titles and body text are dark grey #545454; footer text is light grey #A6A6A6, with the author name in bold. Typefaces: MessinaModern (a transitional-style serif) in Regular and SemiBold for the title, subtitle, circle headings and body, and MessinaSans in Bold and Book (a sans-serif) for the footer. Title is about 5.6% of slide height (roughly 30pt on a 7.5in-high slide), subtitle and body about 2.6–3.0% (roughly 14–16pt), circle headings about 3.3% (about 18pt), footer about 1.9% (about 10pt). Shape outlines are plain, with no corner rounding because they are circles; arrows are solid flat triangles.

**Layout**

Three overlapping browser-window screenshots fan across the middle of the slide in a staggered diagonal, rising from lower-left to upper-right, beneath a left-aligned title block. The title and subtitle sit in the upper-left area starting near x 20.5%, with the windows occupying the lower two-thirds. Footer text is right-aligned in the bottom-right corner, with the author name bold, then the date, then the slide number, all on one baseline near y 91%. No header bar or logo sits outside the windows.

**Visuals**

Three screenshot mock-ups of news web pages, each in a macOS-style browser window with red, yellow and green traffic-light buttons at top-left, a grey rounded address bar with a reload icon, and a soft drop shadow. Back to front: CNBC Make It window (right, top edge about y 18.7%, cut off on its left by the Business Insider window), Fortune window (left, top edge about y 16.2%, its right side covered by the Business Insider window), and Business Insider window (centre, front-most, top edge about y 18.7%). Fortune window: white page with a red Subscribe button, black condensed FORTUNE wordmark and hamburger icon; a red headline kicker; a serif headline; below it a photograph of hands typing on a laptop keyboard with glowing blue holographic icons, robot heads and a large glowing 'Ai' cube, blue-toned, cropped at the window bottom. Business Insider window: white page with black BUSINESS INSIDER wordmark and black circular hamburger button, a grey-green stock ticker strip, a small AI label, a large black sans headline, a blue byline link and outlined Follow pill; below is a photograph of a man in a dark navy suit and light blue shirt with glasses and curly hair, gesturing with one hand and speaking into a microphone, set against a royal-blue World Economic Forum backdrop with repeated white logo text, eye-level medium shot, cropped at the window bottom. CNBC Make It window: white page with a small CNBC peacock logo and bold 'make it' wordmark, a dark rule under the header, a purple INVESTING kicker, a large dark slate headline, a date line, author names, and red X, LinkedIn and email share icons; below is a photograph of an older man with grey hair in a light checked shirt in profile facing left, lit by a vivid magenta and green backdrop of grid panels, cropped by a teal-green rectangular frame offset to the right and bottom of the image.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 solid fill covering the whole slide. Light grey flat background #F1F1F1 with no texture. Title in a serif face (MessinaModern-Regular, a Modern serif, regular weight) in grey #545454, size about 5.56% of slide height, left-aligned. Subtitle in the same serif, regular, #545454, about 2.59% of slide height. Footer in MessinaSans, Bold for the name and Book for the date and number, #A6A6A6, about 1.85% of slide height. Screenshot text uses bold and condensed sans and serif faces from the source sites: black #111111 headlines, red #E10600 Fortune accents, blue #1A4BD6 Business Insider link, purple #7B61FF CNBC kicker, slate #3B4A5A CNBC headline. Browser windows have white #FFFFFF chrome with rounded corners of about 2% of slide width and soft grey drop shadows; traffic-light dots are red #FF5F57, yellow #FEBC2E and green #28C840. A teal #00F0C8 rectangular accent sits behind the CNBC photo's lower right corner. No gradients.

### Slide 8

**Text**

This often brings bubbles
People draw straight lines on log scale charts
They forget that exponential growth is
generally an exponential curve
And always say “this is different”
The trouble is, they’re generally right - every
bubble is different! But it can still be a bubble
Benedict Evans –– November 2025 8

**Layout**

Two-column split: a narrow-tinted text panel fills the left half (grey #F2F2F2) and a white right half holds a single book cover photo, centred horizontally at about x 60 to 87 and y 5 to 85. The title sits top-left of the text block at y 25.6, left-aligned at x 4.6 with a left margin of about 4.6 per cent; body lines follow beneath it with paragraph gaps of about 8 per cent of slide height. The footer runs along the bottom right at y 91.1 with author name, date and slide number on one baseline; the slide number sits at x 95.0, right margin about 4 per cent.

**Visuals**

One photograph-style illustration: the cover of the book “This Time Is Different” by Carmen M. Reinhart and Kenneth S. Rogoff, shown as a portrait book cover (about 410 by 635 px, aspect roughly 0.65) placed at x 61.6, y 8.0, w 26.9, h 74.1 with a soft grey drop shadow beneath it (a larger 579 by 862 px image at x 59.9, y 5.3, w 30.1, h 79.8 supplies the shadow). The cover is not cropped, not full-bleed, and is boxed with square corners. Cover subject: a flat vector illustration of a black-suited businessman in mid-fall with arms outspread, legs bent, a white shirt and dark tie, plus a dark briefcase and white papers tumbling from the upper right. A large white torn-looking sheet sits in the upper left carrying the title. Lighting is flat and even with a faint vignette darker toward the edges and bottom. Dominant colours: olive khaki gradient background about #A7A578 lightening to #B5B48A at the top, white sheet #FFFFFF, black figure #1A1A1A and grey shading #555555. Overlay: none. Cover text: “NEW YORK TIMES BESTSELLER” in white serif capitals with the first words italic; “Winner of the 2010 TIAA-CREF Paul A. Samuelson Award” in small white serif; “THIS TIME IS DIFFERENT” in large black condensed sans capitals on the white sheet; “Eight Centuries of Financial Folly” in italic serif; “CARMEN M. REINHART & KENNETH S. ROGOFF” in black condensed sans capitals; and “‘A masterpiece.’ —Martin Wolf, Financial Times” in small white serif at lower left.

**Design**

Background: Split background: left half solid #F2F2F2 light grey, right half solid #FFFFFF white. Two flat background panels with no gradients on the slide itself: left panel light grey #F2F2F2 and right panel pure white #FFFFFF, meeting at a vertical split at x 50. Title and body text colour #545454 dark grey. Footer text colour #A6A6A6 light grey. No accent colour on the slide. Typefaces: MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional serif (Messina Modern) in regular weight for the title and body; MessinaSans-Bold and MessinaSans-Book, a neutral grotesque sans-serif, for the footer. Title size about 5.56 per cent of slide height; body about 3.33 per cent; footer about 1.85 per cent. Line spacing is about 1.2 for wrapped body lines. The book cover has square corners and a soft diffuse drop shadow of about 1 to 2 per cent of slide height offset downward. No borders, no rules.

**Layout**

A full-width light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5% of the height) holds a left-aligned serif title and subtitle starting at x 20.5%, with the title at y 5.6% and the subtitle at y 13.3%. Below it a white panel (y 20.5% to 86.1%) holds a single line chart with its chart title at x 20.6%, y 27.4%, and the plot area spanning roughly x 20.5% to 95.3%. A light grey footer band (y 86.1% to 100%) holds the left-aligned source line at x 20.5%, y 90.9%, and on the right the author name, date and page number 9 at x 94.9%, y 91.1%, all on one baseline.

**Visuals**

One line chart. Type: line chart with a single smooth red series. Title: 'US heterosexual couples who met online, by year of meeting'. Y axis: percentage, 0% to 60%, gridlines and labels every 10%. X axis: years, labelled 1995, 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015 and 2020, with the line running from 1995 to about 2022. No values are printed, so estimates are marked 'about': the series starts at about 2% in 1995, is about 10% in 2000, about 19% in 2005, about 20% in 2010, about 32% in 2015, about 48% in 2020, and ends at about 59% around 2022. Legend: none. Highlighted points: none. Series line is red (#E8473B) about 3 px thick with no markers.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 covering the full slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Flat, minimal look with no gradients, shadows or rounded corners. Background light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands, with a white #FFFFFF central panel. Title and subtitle are #545454 in a Messina Modern style serif, regular weight. Chart title and tick labels are #545454 or #545554 in Messina Sans. Only accent is the red data line #E8473B. Footer author name is bold #A6A6A6, date and page number are book weight #A6A6A6. Title font is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, chart title about 2.2%, axis labels and footer about 1.85%. Gridlines are thin dotted grey #A6A6A6 lines; the 0% baseline is a solid thin grey line.

### Slide 10

**Text**

New platforms mean new tools (and new revenue)
SaaS means the typical large enterprise in the USA now uses 4-500 apps
Mainframes
One app
PCs
Dozens of apps
SaaS
4-500 apps*
LLMs
What can we automate next?
*Source: Productiv
Benedict Evans –– November 2025 10

**Layout**

Four circles sit in one horizontal row across the middle of the slide, joined by three small grey triangular arrows, with the content block starting at about 20% from the left edge and leaving the left fifth of the slide empty. The title is left-aligned at the top, with a one-line subtitle beneath it, and a source line sits bottom-left with the footer (author, date and page number) bottom-right on a shared baseline near 91% of the slide height.

**Visuals**

Four filled circles in a left-to-right process flow, with three grey right-pointing triangle arrows between them. Circle 1 (dark grey) reads Mainframes / One app. Circle 2 (dark grey) reads PCs / Dozens of apps. Circle 3 (dark grey) reads SaaS / 4-500 apps*. Circle 4 (red) reads LLMs / What can we automate next?. Each arrow is a solid isosceles triangle pointing right, about 2% of slide width and 7% of slide height, placed in the gap between adjacent circles at about 50% to 57% of slide height. No photographs, charts or logos.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Flat minimal style with no gradients, shadows or outlines. Background light grey #F1F1F1. Title and subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional serif, weight regular, colour #545454. Title is about 5.6% of slide height; subtitle about 2.6%. Circle labels in MessinaModern-Book serif, white #FFFFFF, about 3.0% of slide height, with the first line of each circle at the same size as the second. Circles are dark grey #535353 except the fourth, which is red #EB483F. Arrows are light grey #A6A6A6. Source line is MessinaSans-Regular in #545454 at about 1.9%. Footer is MessinaSans in #A6A6A6, with the author name in Bold and the date and page number in Book, all about 1.9%.

### Slide 11

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: Two large quotes on a plain background make a single point land with strong typographic hierarchy and generous white space.

**Text**

One way this platform shift is different, though / For PCs, the web or smartphones, we knew the / physical limits of what could happen next year / With LLMs, we don’t know how much better / this could get / “The race to AGI is afoot” / Sergey Brin / “AGI needs multiple / further breakthroughs” / Demis Hassabis / Benedict Evans –– November 2025 11

**Layout**

Two-column split slide: a grey left panel occupies the left half (0 to 50% width) and a white right panel the right half. Left column text is left-aligned at about 4.6% x, with the title at the top of the text block around 25.6% y, followed by a two-paragraph body. Right column quotations are left-aligned at about 56.7% x, vertically centred in the panel, with an attribution beneath each quote. Footer sits bottom-right at about 91% y, with the author name, dash and date, and the slide number at the far right (about 94.7% x).

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background split: left panel light grey #F2F2F2 over the left half, right panel white #FFFFFF over the right half. Titles and body text on the left are dark grey #545454 in a serif typeface resembling MessinaModern (Regular weight), with the title set in a larger size than body. The title's word 'is' is set in italic. Right-hand quotations are set in a serif resembling MessinaModern Book in red #EB483F, larger than the left body text. Footer text is a sans-serif resembling MessinaSans, in light grey #A6A6A6; author name in bold, date and slide number in book weight. Title is about 5.6% of slide height (cap height about 0.7 of that), body about 3.3%, footer about 1.9%. Line spacing is tight on the title and quotes, slightly looser on body. No shapes, borders, shadows or gradients.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

- shape at x 50, y 0, w 50, h 100 [#FFFFFF]: Right white panel (Full-height rectangle filling the right half of the slide, no border, no shadow.)

### Slide 12

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: It repeats the timeline and adds a question-mark branch so the open future is shown visually rather than stated in text.
- What would change the score: I would replace the empty left margin on slides 2 and 12 with usable space, shrink the quote-only slides' white boxes, and add direct labels or takeaways on the busiest multi-series charts.

**Text**

Another platform shift, or more?
We know this will get better, but we don’t know how much
?
Mainframes
PCs
Web
Smartphones
Generative AI
Benedict Evans
–– November 2025
12

**Layout**

Full-width header band on a light grey field: the title sits top-left at about 20.5% of slide width with the subtitle directly beneath it, both left-aligned on the same left edge. Below, a full-width white band spans y 20.5% to 86.1%, divided into five white column panels (Mainframes, PCs, Web, Smartphones, Generative AI) separated by thin light grey gutters, with the column labels centred near the bottom of the band. A footer line sits at y 91.1% on the grey field, with the author name, date and page number 12 in the lower right; there is no visible logo.

**Visuals**

Five grey curved arrows (stroke #808080, about 0.4% of slide height, arrowheads filled #808080) step upward from left to right, one per platform: Mainframes curve from about (20.0%, 68.3%) rising to an arrowhead at about (34.8%, 56.6%); PCs curve from about (35.1%, 62.5%) to an arrowhead at about (49.8%, 50.7%); Web curve from about (50.1%, 56.5%) to an arrowhead at about (64.9%, 44.8%); Smartphones curve from about (65.2%, 50.7%) to an arrowhead at about (80.0%, 38.9%). Each curve is an S-shaped bezier, flattening at its start and end. In the Generative AI column, a red (#EB483F) dotted arrow rises steeply from about (80.4%, 43.6%) to an arrowhead at about (80.9%, 2.0%), leaving the top edge of the slide; a second red dotted arrow leaves the same origin, runs gently right and up in an S-curve to an arrowhead at about (95.1%, 31.7%). A red question mark sits between the two red arrows at about (83.3%, 36.5%). No photographs, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Flat, minimal look with no gradients, shadows or corner radii. Grey field #F1F1F1 at the top and bottom with white #FFFFFF column band. Title and subtitle in grey #545454 serif (Messina Modern style, a Scotch-like book serif), title regular weight about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%. Column labels in grey #545454 serif Messina Modern Book about 3.0%; the Generative AI label and question mark use accent red #EB483F. Footer in #A6A6A6 sans-serif (Messina Sans): author name bold about 1.9%, date and page number book weight. Lines are 0.4% stroke width, grey #808080 for the arrows and red #EB483F dotted for the two Generative AI arrows.

No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

### Slide 16

**Text**

Three years of FOMO in Big Tech
“This is a huge new market, a huge threat to all our existing businesses, and we can’t miss it”
“This is a huge / opportunity and / threat / We can’t miss it”
“It keeps / getting better / with more / capex”
How much / better? / How much / capex?
Benedict Evans –– November 2025 16

**Layout**

Single slide with a title and subtitle block top-left aligned to a common left edge at about 20.5% of slide width, with three equal-sized circles in a horizontal row across the middle-lower area, separated by two small chevron arrows, and a footer line at bottom right. The title sits at the top, the subtitle directly beneath it, the circles are vertically centred in the slide body, and the footer occupies the bottom right margin with the page number at the far right edge.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles connected left to right by two small grey right-pointing triangular chevrons. Circle 1 is dark grey #535353 containing the white serif text “This is a huge opportunity and threat / We can’t miss it”. Circle 2 is dark grey #535353 containing the white serif text “It keeps getting better with more capex”. Circle 3 is red #EB483F containing the white serif text “How much better? / How much capex?”. Each chevron is a solid light grey #A6A6A6 isosceles triangle pointing right, positioned in the gap between circles, vertically centred on the circles. Circles are equal in size, about 22.1% of slide width and 39.3% of slide height, with no shadows, gradients or outlines. No photographs, charts or logos.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 covering the entire slide.

### Slide 17

**Review notes**

- Worth studying: A clean stacked chart with direct labels and one headline statement lets the capex surge carry the argument without extra text.

**Text**

FOMO drives a capex surge
~$400bn in 2025 for the big four alone (for comparison, global telecoms is ~$300bn)
Capex ($bn)
Coreweave
Oracle
Meta
Alphabet
AWS*
Microsoft
500
400
300
200
100
0
2010 2011 2012 2013 2014 2015 2016 2017 2018 2019 2020 2021 2022 2023 2024 2025e
Source: Companies, company guidance. Includes capital leases
* Amazon does not break out AWS capex directly
Benedict Evans –– November 2025 17

**Layout**

Two-band slide: a light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5% of height) holds the title and subtitle, a white body band (20.5% to 86%) holds the chart, and a light grey footer band (86% to 100%) holds the source notes at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned on a shared left edge at x 20.5%. The legend sits in a column at the far left (x 4.8%) and the chart's y-axis labels sit at x 17.8% to 19.0%. The footer source text is aligned to the same x 20.5% edge as the title, and the page number sits at the right margin near x 94.6% to 95.6%.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked vertical bar chart, one bar per year, 2010 to 2024 plus 2025e (estimate). Title printed above the axis: Capex ($bn). Y-axis runs from 0 to 500 in steps of 100 with light grey dotted horizontal gridlines at each step; the 500 gridline is at about 500 and the top of the plot is about 550. X-axis labels are the years 2010, 2011, 2012, 2013, 2014, 2015, 2016, 2017, 2018, 2019, 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025e. Six stacked series, from bottom to top: Microsoft (red #EE4B3E), AWS* (salmon #F4A29C), Alphabet (pale pink #F2D6D2), Meta (black #000000), Oracle (dark grey #4F4F4F), Coreweave (mid grey #A6A6A6). Thin white separators sit between segments. No data values are printed on the bars. Approximate totals: 2010 about 5, 2011 about 6, 2012 about 8, 2013 about 17, 2014 about 20, 2015 about 22, 2016 about 35, 2017 about 38, 2018 about 62, 2019 about 64, 2020 about 63, 2021 about 97, 2022 about 128, 2023 about 140, 2024 about 245, 2025e about 438. Estimated 2025e segments: Microsoft about 118, AWS* about 102, Alphabet about 92, Meta about 73, Oracle about 32, Coreweave about 19. Estimated 2024 segments: Microsoft about 76, AWS* about 57, Alphabet about 55, Meta about 37, Oracle about 10, Coreweave about 10. Legend (top to bottom, square swatches): Coreweave, Oracle, Meta, Alphabet, AWS*, Microsoft. No bars are highlighted; the 2025e bar is the tallest and is the last bar at the right.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF body band in the middle (full-bleed, no image). Flat, minimal style with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners. Background is light grey #F1F1F1 at top and bottom with a white #FFFFFF middle band. Title is a high-contrast serif in regular weight (Messina Modern style, close to a Didone-influenced transitional serif), colour grey #545454, at about 5.6% of slide height for the title. Subtitle is the same serif at about 2.6% of slide height, colour #545454. All other text is a geometric sans-serif (Messina Sans style, similar to Helvetica-like grotesque) in regular weight at about 1.85% of slide height, colour #545454 or #535353; the axis label Capex ($bn) is semibold at about 2.2%, colour #545554. Footer author name is bold #A6A6A6, date and page number are book weight #A6A6A6. Bars have no outlines beyond thin white separators. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey. Axis baseline is a thin grey line.

**Layout**

Single-column slide with a grey top band holding a left-aligned serif title at about 20.5% from the left and a serif subtitle beneath it; a white band occupies the middle with a chart spanning from about 20.5% to 95% horizontally, with a legend column at the far left starting near 4.8%. A grey footer band sits at the bottom with the source note left-aligned at 20.5% and the author, date and page number right-aligned near 95%. Margins are about 20% on the left for content and about 5% on the right; no logo.

**Visuals**

Stacked vertical bar chart titled 'Capex ($bn)'. Y axis: capex in $bn, linear scale from 0 to 500 with labelled gridlines at 0, 100, 200, 300, 400 and 500, drawn as thin dotted light grey horizontal lines. X axis: categories 2010, 2011, 2012, 2013, 2014, 2015, 2016, 2017, 2018, 2019, 2020, 2021, 2022, 2023, 2024, 2025e, Jan est for 2025, with a thin grey baseline at 0. Series stacked bottom to top: Microsoft (red #EE4B3E), Alphabet (salmon #F4A29E), AWS* (pale pink #F2D4D2), Meta (black #000000), Oracle (dark grey #545454), Coreweave (mid grey #A6A6A6). Approximate stacked totals (about, no values printed): 2010 about 5; 2011 about 7; 2012 about 8; 2013 about 17; 2014 about 20; 2015 about 22; 2016 about 35; 2017 about 38; 2018 about 62; 2019 about 65; 2020 about 63; 2021 about 97; 2022 about 128; 2023 about 140; 2024 about 244; 2025e about 437; Jan est for 2025 about 325. Microsoft segment about: 2021 about 27; 2022 about 28; 2023 about 41; 2024 about 76; 2025e about 118; Jan est about 87. Legend at left, top to bottom: Coreweave, Oracle, Meta, AWS*, Alphabet, Microsoft, each with a small filled square. No highlighted points; bars have thin white separators between segments.

**Design**

Background: Solid #F1F1F1 light grey top and bottom bands with a white #FFFFFF middle band. Light grey background #F1F1F1 for top and bottom bands with a white middle band #FFFFFF. Title in a serif face resembling MessinaModern-Regular, regular weight, colour #545454, large (about 5.6% of slide height). Subtitle in the same serif, about 2.6% of height, #545454. Chart labels and legend in a sans-serif face resembling MessinaSans-Regular, about 1.85% of height, #535353 to #545554; the 'Capex ($bn)' heading is MessinaSans-SemiBold about 2.2%. Source note in MessinaSans-Regular #545454; author name in MessinaSans-Bold #A6A6A6; date and page number in MessinaSans-Book #A6A6A6. Accents are the red and pink palette of the bars. Gridlines are thin dotted grey; no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Layout**

Two-band layout on a light grey field: a light grey (#F1F1F1) header band across the top about 20% of the height holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at x about 20.5% so they share a left edge with the chart title, source line and NB line. A white band fills the middle from about 20.5% to 86% of the height and holds a single full-width line chart with a legend column at the far left (x about 4.9%) and the plot area from x about 20.5% to 95.4%. A grey footer strip sits below, with the source lines at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number right-aligned to the right margin near x 95%.

**Visuals**

Chart: multi-series line chart, titled 'US construction value (2025 $bn, seasonally adjusted annual rate)'. Y axis: 0 to 125 in $bn, labelled ticks at 0, 25, 50, 75, 100, 125, with thin dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled tick. X axis: monthly time series from Jan 1995 to Jan 2025, with tick labels at Jan 1995, Jan 2000, Jan 2005, Jan 2010, Jan 2015, Jan 2020, Jan 2025; a thin solid grey baseline at 0. Series (no printed values, estimates marked about): Offices (dark charcoal #4D4D4D, solid 2 px line): starts about 53 in 1995, peaks about 116 in early 2000, falls to about 34 around 2010, rises to about 95 around 2019, ends about 47 in Jan 2025. Retail (mid grey #AAAAAA): starts about 45, about 58 in 2001, peaks about 85 around 2007, falls to about 20 in 2021, ends about 23. Warehouses * (light salmon #F4A6A6): starts about 17, peaks about 31 around 2001, about 9 around 2010, rises to about 84 around 2023, ends about 49. Data centres ** (red #E8362D, thicker line): begins about 2 in early 2014, about 12 in 2020, rises steeply to about 42 by Jan 2025. Legend: four entries at top-left of the plot, each a short coloured line sample followed by its label: Offices (charcoal), Retail (grey), Warehouses * (salmon), Data centres ** (red). No highlighted points or annotations.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of the height. Light grey background #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF chart band. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, regular weight, colour #545454, approximately 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, about 2.6% of height, #545454. Chart title in MessinaSans-SemiBold, about 2.2% of height, #545554. Axis labels, legend and footer text in MessinaSans-Regular, about 1.85% of height, #545454 to #535353. Author name in MessinaSans-Bold and the date and page number in MessinaSans-Book, all #A6A6A6. Lines are thin with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners; gridlines are dotted light grey.

**Layout**

Two horizontal bands frame the slide: a light grey header band (top about 20.5%) holding the title and subtitle, a white content panel in the middle (y 20.5% to 86.1%), and a light grey footer band (y 86.1% to 100%). Title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.5%, which is also the left edge of the chart plot area. A legend sits in the left margin at x 4.8%, outside the chart. The source line sits at x 20.5% in the footer; the author, date and page number are right-aligned in the footer, ending at about x 95.6%. Page number 20 is at the far right of the footer.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled Quarterly revenue ($bn). Y axis: quarterly revenue in US$ billions, linear scale from 0 to 60, gridlines and labels every 10 (0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 60), light dotted grey gridlines, baseline at 0 drawn as a thin solid grey line. X axis: time in years, labels 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020, 2025, quarterly data points from 2000 to about the end of 2025. Series 1, Intel, dark grey line about 2 px wide: about 8 in 2000, dips to about 6.5 in 2001 to 2003, about 10 in 2006, about 14 in 2008, about 7 in 2009, about 11 in 2011, about 14 to 15 in 2014 to 2015, about 20 peak around 2019 to 2020, about 20 in 2021, about 20.5 peak around early 2022, falls to about 15 in 2022, about 12 low in early 2023, about 13 to 15 in 2023 to 2025, about 13 at the end. Series 2, Nvidia, red line about 2 px wide: about 0.2 in 2000 to 2002, about 1 in 2008, about 1 in 2010, about 2 in 2015, about 3 in 2018, about 2.5 in 2019, about 3 in 2020, about 5 in 2021, about 8.5 peak in early 2022, falls to about 6 in 2022, about 7 in early 2023, then rises steeply to about 19 in early 2024, about 30 mid-2024, about 44 late 2024, about 47 in early 2025, and about 57 at the last point in late 2025. Legend: at upper left outside the plot, Intel with short dark grey line sample, Nvidia with short red line sample, stacked vertically. No highlighted points or annotations. No values are printed on the data lines other than the axis labels.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Light grey header and footer bands (#F1F1F1) with a white middle panel (#FFFFFF). Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, regular weight, colour #545454, large. Subtitle in the same serif, regular, #545454, smaller. Chart title and axis labels in MessinaSans (sans-serif), regular weight, #545454; chart title is SemiBold. Source line in MessinaSans Regular #545454. Author name in MessinaSans Bold #A6A6A6; date and page number in MessinaSans Book #A6A6A6. Accent colours: Intel series #545454 dark grey; Nvidia series red about #E8453C. Gridlines light grey dotted about #BDBDBD, 1 px. Shapes have no corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 21

**Text**

Nvidia can’t keep up (neither can TSMC) / TSMC unwilling/unable to expand capacity fast enough to meet Nvidia’s book / Quarterly revenue ($bn) / Intel / Nvidia / TSMC / 60 / 50 / 40 / 30 / 20 / 10 / 0 / 2000 / 2005 / 2010 / 2015 / 2020 / 2025 / Source: Companies / Benedict Evans / –– November 2025 / 21

**Layout**

Full-bleed slide with a light grey (#F1F1F1) band across the top (0 to about 20.5% of height) and bottom (about 86% to 100%), and a white content panel between them. The title sits left-aligned at x 20.5% near the top of the grey header, with the subtitle directly beneath it; the chart block starts at x about 20.5% and spans to about 95% of width, with the legend in the left margin at x about 4.8%. The source line sits bottom-left at x 20.5% and the author, date and page number sit bottom-right in the grey footer band, the page number at x 94.5%.

**Visuals**

One line chart titled Quarterly revenue ($bn), with no chart title inside the plot beyond that label. Type: multi-series line chart with solid and dashed lines, no markers. Y axis: linear, $bn, range 0 to 60, labelled ticks 0, 10, 20, 30, 40, 50, 60, with light grey dotted horizontal gridlines at each tick and a solid thin grey baseline at 0. X axis: time, quarterly, 2000 to about 2025, labelled every 5 years (2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020, 2025), no axis title. Series: Intel, dark grey (#545454) solid line, about 8 in 2000, about 6.5 in 2001, about 7 in 2002, about 10 in 2005, about 10.5 in 2008, about 7.5 in 2009, about 10 in 2010, about 13 in 2011, about 13.5 in 2014, about 19 in 2018, about 20 in 2020, about 20.5 peak in 2022, about 15 in 2023, about 12 low in late 2023, about 13 in 2024, about 15.5 in 2024, about 13 at end 2025. Nvidia, red (#E8473F approx.) solid line, about 0.3 in 2000 rising slowly to about 1 in 2010, about 2 in 2016, about 3 in 2018, about 3 in 2020, about 5 in 2021, about 8.5 in 2022, about 6 low in 2023, about 7 in 2023, rising steeply to about 57 at the far right end in 2025, with about 47 just before. TSMC, red (#E8473F approx.) dashed line, about 1 in 2000, about 2 in 2001, about 3 in 2008, about 1 in 2009, about 4 in 2011, about 5 in 2013, about 7 in 2015, about 10 in 2019, about 12.5 in 2021, about 15 in 2022, about 20 peak in 2023, about 16 in 2023 trough, about 20 in 2024, about 27 in 2025, about 30 at the far right end. No legend box; legend is a list of three line-sample keys at left. No highlighted points or annotations.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 covering the full slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel from about 20.5% to 86% of height. Minimal, flat, editorial style. Background light grey #F1F1F1 for header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF middle panel. Title in a high-contrast serif (MessinaModern-Regular, close to a modern serif such as Georgia or Didone-style serif) in dark grey #545454, large, about 5.6% of slide height (cap height about 3.9%). Subtitle in the same serif, regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart label and all axis, legend and source text in a neo-grotesque sans-serif (MessinaSans, similar to Helvetica-type grotesk), regular weight, #545454 or #535353, about 1.85% of slide height. Author name in sans bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in sans book #A6A6A6. Accent colour: red #E8473F for Nvidia and TSMC lines; Intel line dark grey #545454. Lines are about 2 px wide at 2000 px width for solid series, dashed series with equal dashes. Gridlines are thin dotted grey #A6A6A6-style. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

**Layout**

Full-width light grey (#F1F1F1) slide with a white band across the middle holding the chart; the title and subtitle sit top-left aligned at about x 20.5% in a grey header band, the chart title and chart start at the same left edge, and a source line sits at the bottom left with the author, date and slide number right-aligned in the footer band. The chart's legend sits at the far left margin above the value axis, and the slide number 22 is at the right margin.

**Visuals**

One stacked vertical bar chart, titled 'Main constraints to data centre construction, USA (February 2025)'. Eight categories on the x-axis, left to right: Utility power, Access to chips, Fibre, Permitting, Water availability, Access to natural gas, Land, Public opposition. Y-axis: percentage of respondents, 0% to 100%, gridlines (dotted, light grey) at 25%, 50%, 75% and 100%. Three stacked series, bottom to top: Extremely significant (red #EB483F), Very significant (pale pink #FBDAD9), Somewhat significant (grey #7F7F7F). Values are not printed; estimates marked about from bar heights: Utility power about 40 / 33 / 19 (total about 92); Access to chips about 32 / 34 / 15 (total about 81); Fibre about 26 / 33 / 26 (total about 85); Permitting about 26 / 30 / 30 (total about 86); Water availability about 21 / 20 / 32 (total about 73); Access to natural gas about 19 / 23 / 34 (total about 76); Land about 17 / 30 / 27 (total about 74); Public opposition about 16 / 25 / 33 (total about 74). Legend at upper left of chart, stacked vertically in order top to bottom: Somewhat significant, Very significant, Extremely significant, each with a small solid square swatch. No highlighted points. Bars are evenly spaced, about 6.2% of slide width wide, with thin white separators between segments. Source: Schneider Electric industry survey.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 with a white band (#FFFFFF) across the middle of the slide. Light grey background #F1F1F1 with a white central band; title in a grey serif (Messina Modern style, regular weight) #545454, body and axis text in a light geometric sans (Messina Sans, regular and bold) #545454, footer names in bold grey #A6A6A6. Title size about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, chart title about 2.2% semibold, axis and legend labels about 1.85%. Accents are red #EB483F with pale pink #FBDAD9 and mid grey #7F7F7F. Lines are thin dotted light grey gridlines; the category axis is a thin solid grey baseline. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 23

**Text**

“It’s been almost impossible to build / capacity fast enough since ChatGPT / launched” / Kevin Scott, Microsoft CTO / Benedict Evans –– November 2025 / 23

**Layout**

A single white content panel sits on a light grey page, with the quote block left-aligned inside the panel near its top-left area, leaving the lower and right portions of the panel empty. The panel spans roughly x 17.4% to 95.6% and y 24.9% to 86.1%, with the grey margin visible on all sides. The footer line sits bottom-right below the panel, with author name, date and slide number right-aligned on one baseline.

**Visuals**

none

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F2F2F2 covering the entire slide. Light grey page background #F2F2F2 with a flat white rectangle #FFFFFF panel, no border, no shadow, no rounded corners. **Visuals**

Chart: clustered column chart titled 'Global data centre capacity estimates, H1 2025 (GW)'. X axis: seven categories, labels Omdia/IEA, Bloomberg BNEF, Goldman Sachs, Morgan Stanley, Bain, BCG, McKinsey, shown with a thin grey baseline at 0. Est. of 2030 plans (about): Omdia/IEA about 195, Bloomberg BNEF about 221, Goldman Sachs about 149, Morgan Stanley about 250, Bain about 163, BCG about 169, McKinsey about 218. Legend at upper left of chart: dark grey square 'Current' above red square 'Est. of 2030 plans'. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Title in a high-contrast serif (Messina Modern style, regular weight) in #545454, about 5.6% of slide height for cap-height line; subtitle in the same serif, about 2.6% of slide height, #545454. Chart title and legend in a humanist sans (Messina Sans style) semibold for chart title, regular for legend and axis labels, #535353 to #545554, about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height. ### Slide 27

**Text**

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) slide with a white chart band spanning the middle from about 20% to 86% of the height. Title and subtitle are left-aligned on a common left edge at about 20.5% of the width, with the chart title on the same edge. The chart plot runs from about 20.5% to 95.4% of the width, with a legend at top-left in the margin at about 3% to 11%. Source line sits bottom-left on the same edge as the title; the author, date and slide number sit bottom-right, the slide number at about 94.4% of the width.

**Visuals**

Chart: clustered-stacked column chart titled 'Annual Capex and Free Cash Flow, 2010 to 2025e ($bn)'. Four company groups along the x-axis, left to right: Alphabet, Amazon, Meta, Microsoft, each with about 16 annual columns (2010 to 2025e, calendar years, oldest at left). Each column is a stacked pair: grey FCF segment at the bottom and red Cash capex segment on top. Y-axis in $bn, labelled 200, 150, 100, 50, 0, -50, dotted gridlines at 200, 150, 100 and 50, a solid baseline at -50 and a faint dotted zero line; scale approx 1.97 px per $bn. Legend: red square 'Cash capex', dark grey square 'FCF', stacked top-left of the plot. Values are estimates, as the slide prints no bar values. Alphabet: final (2025e) column about 161 total, FCF about 69, capex about 92; earlier columns rise from about 10 total in 2010 to about 91 by 2023. Amazon: early columns near 0 to 10; a dip with FCF about -20 in the two columns before the final years (about 2022 and 2023); final column about 135 total with FCF about 10 and capex about 125. Meta: columns rise from near 0 to a final column about 128 total, FCF about 60, capex about 68. Microsoft: columns rise from about 25 to a final column about 155 total, FCF about 74, capex about 81. No highlighted points. Flat design, no shadows or gradients.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 with a white band #FFFFFF behind the chart. Title in a high-contrast serif (MessinaModern-Regular, a modern serif, regular weight) in charcoal #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, regular weight, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis labels in a geometric grotesque sans (MessinaSans, regular, with SemiBold for the chart title and Bold for the author name) in #545454 and #535353, about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height. Accent colour is red #EC4B3B for Cash capex; FCF is charcoal #545454. Footer text in light grey #A6A6A6. Gridlines are thin dotted grey #A6A6A6; baseline solid grey #8C8C8C. Bars have thin white separators. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 28

**Text**

The hyperscalers can afford it… up to a point / Capital leases are not new, but they’ve got a lot bigger / Annual Capex and Free Cash Flow, 2010 to 2025e ($bn) / Leases / Cash capex / FCF / 200 / 150 / 100 / 50 / 0 / -50 / Alphabet / Amazon / Meta / Microsoft / Source: Companies, consensus estimates. Calendar year. / Benedict Evans / –– November 2025 / 28

**Layout**

Two horizontal bands: a light grey header band across the top (about 0 to 20.5% of slide height) holding the title and subtitle, then a white content panel (about 20.5% to 86% height) with the chart, then a light grey footer band (about 86% to 100%) with the source line at left and author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned on a shared left edge at about 20.5% of slide width, leaving a wide left margin; the legend sits in the left margin at about 4.8% x. The chart spans from about 18% to 95% of width. Page number 28 sits at bottom right, about 94% x.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked column chart titled Annual Capex and Free Cash Flow, 2010 to 2025e ($bn). Y axis labelled in $bn with gridlines (dotted, light grey) at 200, 150, 100, 50, 0 and -50, range about -50 to 200; a solid grey zero baseline sits at 0 with a darker axis line at the bottom at -50. X axis groups by company: Alphabet, Amazon, Meta, Microsoft, each group a run of thin adjacent columns representing successive calendar years from 2010 to 2025e. Each column is stacked: dark grey FCF at the bottom, mid grey Cash capex above it, red Leases on top where present; columns are separated by thin white gaps. Alphabet columns rise from about 10 in early years to about 160 total in the last column (about 69 FCF, about 91 cash capex on top, leases none). Amazon columns rise from near 0 to about 135 total in the last column (about 10 FCF, about 125 cash capex), with negative FCF bars below zero of about -20 in two years. Meta columns rise from near 0 to about 128 total in the last column (about 60 FCF, about 68 cash capex). Microsoft columns rise from about 25 to about 194 total in the last column (about 74 FCF, about 81 cash capex, about 39 leases in red at the top); the Microsoft leases segments are visible from about 2023 onward, with the last few columns showing red segments of roughly 5 to 39. Legend at upper left of chart: red square Leases, mid grey square Cash capex, dark grey square FCF, stacked vertically. No highlighted points beyond the red lease segments. Values other than the printed axis ticks are estimates marked about.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 band across the top and bottom with a white #FFFFFF panel in the middle. Light grey header and footer bands in #F1F1F1 framing a white content panel #FFFFFF. Title in a serif modern typeface similar to Messina Modern Regular, regular weight, dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and all axis and legend labels in a humanist sans-serif similar to Messina Sans, chart title SemiBold #545554 about 2.2% of slide height, tick and category labels Regular #545454 about 1.85%. Bars: FCF dark charcoal #535353, Cash capex mid grey about #A6A6A6, Leases red about #E84A3F. Gridlines dotted light grey. Footer source line Regular #545454 about 1.85%; author name Bold #A6A6A6; date and page number Book #A6A6A6, about 1.85%. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

Up to a point / Hyperscalers add leases and debt, while some analysts suggest Oracle’s cloud capex might be >100% of revenue / ft.com / FINANCIAL TIMES / © FT montage/The Washington Post/Ge / Big Tech / + Add to myFT / ‘Absolutely immense’: the companies on the hook for the $3tn AI building boom / Private capital joins Big Tech in seeking to capture rewards fro / historic expansion of data centres / wsj.com / WSJ / Oracle Isn’t Answering the Hardest Questions About Its AI Plans / Software giant boosts long-term targets, but paying for an artificial-intelligence build-out will stretch its resources / By Dan Gallagher Follow / Oct. 17, 2025 at 5:30 am ET / reuters.com / Reuters / My News / Meta taps PIMCO, Blue Owl for $29 billion data center expansion project, source says / By Chandni Shah and Surbhi Misra / August 7, 2025 11:10 PM EDT · Updated August 7, 2025 / Aa / A Meta logo is pictured at a trade fair in Hannover Messe, in Hanover, Germany, April 22, 2024. / Benedict Evans –– November 2025 / 29

**Layout**

Three browser-window mockups sit in a loose staggered row across the middle of the slide: the Financial Times window at left, the Reuters window at right, and the Wall Street Journal window in front and centred, slightly lower, overlapping the inner edges of the other two. The title is left-aligned at the top, starting at about x 20.5%, with a one-line subtitle directly beneath it running almost to the right margin; the footer sits bottom right with the author, date and slide number on one baseline at about y 91%.

**Visuals**

Three screenshot mockups of news websites in browser-window frames, each with red, yellow and green traffic-light dots at top left, a grey rounded address bar with a reload icon, and a white or tinted page beneath. Left, ft.com: a peach-coloured Financial Times masthead with a hamburger icon and the FINANCIAL TIMES wordmark in serif capitals; below, a black-and-white photograph of a chain-link fence with a field of earth mounds and a digger behind it, with red diagonal lines overlaid across the image and the credit line '© FT montage/The Washington Post/Getty Images' partly cropped by the WSJ window; the photo is cropped at the window edge. Then 'Big Tech' in red, an outlined pill button '+ Add to myFT', a bold serif headline in three lines, a short black rule, and a sans-serif standfirst; the peach panel is a pale salmon tone. Middle, wsj.com: a white window with a hamburger icon and the WSJ serif wordmark centred; bold serif headline in two lines, a grey sans standfirst, a blue italic byline 'Dan Gallagher' with an outlined 'Follow' button, grey italic dateline, and a row of five grey line icons (share, bookmark, text size 'AA', comment bubble, headphones). Below is a photograph of a man with light hair in a dark navy sweater sitting in a dark armchair on a stage, gesturing with one hand, with a small green side table bearing a plant and water bottle at left; behind him is a large red ring-shaped stage backdrop on a deep magenta-purple background; lighting is warm stage light, the image is full-width within the window and cropped at the bottom edge. Right, reuters.com: a white window with the orange dotted Reuters logo and wordmark at left, 'My News' text, a magnifier icon and hamburger icon at right; bold sans headline in two lines, grey byline with names in bold, grey dateline, three outlined icon buttons (bookmark, 'Aa', share), and below a photograph of the Meta logo: a blue infinity-loop symbol and black 'Meta' lettering on a pale beige wall, shallow depth of field, lit softly from the front; the photo is cropped at the window bottom. An orange-red circular floating button with a white sparkle icon overlaps the lower-left corner of the photo, and a small grey caption sits beneath the photo. No logos other than the Reuters, WSJ, FT and Meta marks.

**Design**

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 fill covering the entire slide. Light grey slide with a clean serif title and subtitle in dark grey and a restrained grey sans footer. Title and subtitle use Messina Modern Regular in #545454; the footer uses Messina Sans, bold for the author name and book weight for the date and number, in #A6A6A6. The title is about 5.6% of slide height and the subtitle about 2.6%, the footer about 1.9%. Browser windows are white with rounded corners (about 3% radius), thin light-grey chrome, soft drop shadows beneath them, and traffic-light dots in red #FF5F57, yellow #FEBC2E and green #28C840. The FT panel uses peach #F9DCC6 for its lower section and masthead #FDF0E4; the WSJ and Reuters pages are white with black serif and sans-serif headlines in #111111 and grey secondary text #6B6B6B; blue links #0D6EB0; the Reuters floating button is #E8431E with a white icon.

Single-column stacked layout on a full-bleed light grey field. The title sits top-left at about x 20.5% and the subtitle is directly beneath it. Each band holds a left-aligned statement in the left column starting at x 20.5%, a red dotted arrow at the centre, and a right-aligned-start statement in the right column starting at x 73.3%. A footer row sits at the bottom with the source note at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number at right, ending near x 95.6%.

Three identical horizontal red dotted arrows, one in each white band, vertically centred on each statement pair. Each arrow is a dotted line of small round dots running from about x 49.4% to x 65.0% of slide width, ending in a small solid filled triangular arrowhead pointing right. Arrow 1 at about y 30.3%, arrow 2 at about y 53.1%, arrow 3 at about y 75.7%. Dot colour and arrowhead colour approximately #E8453C red. No photographs, charts, tables, logos or icons.

34

**Layout**

Full-width three-band slide: a light grey header band (top about 20% of the height) holding the title and subtitle, a white central band with the chart, and a light grey footer band with the source line at left and the author, date and page number at right. Title and subtitle are left-aligned on a shared left edge at about x 20.5%, the chart title and y-axis labels sit on the same margin, and the legend sits at the far left at about x 4.8%. The page number 34 sits at the right margin at about x 94.3%, bottom footer line at about y 91%.

**Visuals**

Chart: scatter plot titled 'Models by aggregate benchmark score'. Y axis: aggregate benchmark score, unitless, range 0 to about 80 with labelled ticks at 0, 25, 50 and 75; dotted light grey horizontal gridlines at 25, 50 and 75 and a thin solid grey baseline at 0. X axis: date, labelled ticks at Nov 2022, Nov 2023, Nov 2024 and Nov 2025 with the plot running from Nov 2022 to Nov 2025; small tick marks at the Nov 2023 and Nov 2024 positions. Series 1, 'OpenAI', red circles (about 15 px diameter, white thin outline). Series 2, 'All other', dark grey circles of the same size with white outline. No values are printed; estimates: the lowest OpenAI point is about 9 in Nov 2022; about 22 near early 2023; about 24 near Nov 2023; about 26 near mid 2024; about 29 near late 2023; about 45 and 40 near late 2023; about 47 and 49 around early-mid 2024; about 51 to 69 across late 2024 and 2025 with the highest OpenAI points about 69 and 67 in late 2025. Most non-OpenAI points lie between about 5 and 60, with the densest cluster between about 25 and 50 in 2025. Legend: two entries at top left of the plot, a red dot labelled OpenAI above a dark grey dot labelled All other. Highlighted points: none beyond the colour coding of OpenAI models.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 at top and bottom with a white #FFFFFF central band from about 20.5% to 86.1% of the height. Light grey (#F1F1F1) header and footer bands with a white (#FFFFFF) chart band. Titles and body text are dark grey #545454; the chart title is #545454 in semibold sans. Accent colour for OpenAI points is red #E8473B; all other points are #555555. Title typeface is a transitional serif, close to MessinaModern Regular, regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle is the same serif at about 2.6%. Chart title and labels are a geometric grotesque sans, close to MessinaSans, regular weight at about 1.9% (chart title semibold at 2.2%). Footer author name is bold sans in #A6A6A6, date and page number in book weight #A6A6A6. Lines are thin dotted grey gridlines (#BBBBBB approx.) and a solid grey baseline. No corner radii, shadows or gradients.

**Elements and positions** (back to front; x, y, width, height as % of the slide; type, colour and alignment in brackets)

**Visuals**

Two clustered vertical bar charts sharing one truncated y-axis, with no axis title. Chart title: 'Best scoring model relative to leader, October 2025'. Y-axis: 90% to 100% of leader, labelled 90%, 95% and 100% (the 90% baseline is marked 'NB truncated axis'); gridlines dotted at 100% and 95%; no units beyond percent. Left chart category label 'LMArena Text' with 10 bars, left to right, colour and estimated value: Anthropic red #EB483F about 100%; Google black #000000 about 99.9%; OpenAI grey #7F7F7F about 99.2%; Other: China light grey #DDDDDD about 98.7%; Other: China about 98.2%; Other: Western salmon #F5A39F about 98.0%; Other: China about 97.6%; Other: China about 97.5%; Other: Western about 97.0%; Other: Western about 95.8%. **Layout**

Full-width header band across the top (about 20% of slide height) holds a left-aligned serif title and subtitle starting at about 20.5% from the left edge. A white content area sits below, with a vertical legend at the far left (about 5% from the left) and a single clustered bar chart spanning about 20% to 95% horizontally. A grey footer band holds the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number at right, ending at about 95% from the left edge.

**Visuals**

Clustered vertical bar chart. Title above the plot: 'Weekly active users of generative AI tools as share of the population, June 2025'. Y-axis: percentage of population, 0% to 30%, gridlines (dotted, light grey) at 10%, 20% and 30%, solid baseline at 0%. X-axis: five country categories, USA, UK, France, Denmark, Japan. Each category has six bars in legend order, values read as about: USA: ChatGPT about 22%, Google Gemini about 15%, Meta AI about 11%, Microsoft Copilot about 8%, Grok about 4%, Claude about 2%. UK: ChatGPT about 17%, Google Gemini about 6%, Meta AI about 8%, Microsoft Copilot about 6%, Grok about 1%, Claude about 1%. France: ChatGPT about 18%, Google Gemini about 8%, Meta AI about 4%, Microsoft Copilot about 5%, Grok about 1%, Claude about 1%. Denmark: ChatGPT about 28%, Google Gemini about 7%, Meta AI about 3%, Microsoft Copilot about 6%, Grok about 1%, Claude about 0%. Japan: ChatGPT about 16%, Google Gemini about 13%, Meta AI about 4%, Microsoft Copilot about 7%, Grok about 3%, Claude about 2%. Bars are flat-filled with no outlines, thin white gaps between adjacent bars. Legend (top-left, vertical, square swatches): ChatGPT red, Google Gemini black, Meta AI dark grey, Microsoft Copilot mid grey, Grok light grey, Claude pale pink. No highlighted points. No photographs or logos.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-bleed, with a white #FFFFFF panel covering the middle band from about 20.5% to 86% of slide height. Light grey (#F1F1F1) background band top and bottom with white central chart panel. Title and subtitle in dark grey #545454 serif (Messina Modern style, a regular-weight transitional serif). Chart title and axis labels in grey sans-serif (Messina Sans style, semibold for chart title, regular for labels) #545454 to #535353. Footer author name in bold grey #A6A6A6, date and page number in book-weight grey #A6A6A6. Title size about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, chart and body labels about 1.9%. Accent: ChatGPT bars red #EB483F. Gridlines dotted light grey. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey slide split into three horizontal bands: a grey header band (top about 20% of the slide) holding a left-aligned serif title and subtitle, a white central band (about 20% to 86%) holding a chart titled at left with a line chart spanning roughly x 17% to 95%, and a grey footer band (bottom about 14%) holding a source line at left and author, date and page number at right. Left text edge is at about x 20.5%, and the page number sits at about x 94% to 95%, leaving margins of roughly 5% on the right.

**Visuals**

One line chart with red markers, rendered as a smoothed line. Chart type: line chart with markers, no bars. Chart title: ChatGPT global weekly active users (m). Y-axis: unit millions of users (m), range 0 to 1,000, labelled at 0, 250, 500, 750 and 1,000 with dotted horizontal gridlines at each label. X-axis: time from Dec 2022 to Dec 2025, labelled every six months at Dec 2022, Jun 2023, Dec 2023, Jun 2024, Dec 2024, Jun 2025 and Dec 2025, with a thin grey baseline. Series: one series, weekly active users. No values are printed on the slide, so the estimated values are: Dec 2022 about 0 (marked start point); about Nov 2023 about 100; about Aug 2024 about 200; about Dec 2024 about 300; about Mar 2025 about 400; about Jun 2025 about 700; about Sep 2025 about 800. Highlighted points: every data point is shown as a filled red circle marker, with the last point at about 800 at the top right. No legend. The chart is a placed raster image of 1412 by 423 pixels with a transparent background, sitting on the white band with no box or border.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 with a central full-width white #FFFFFF band from about 20.5% to 86% of slide height. Light grey (#F1F1F1) header and footer bands with a white (#FFFFFF) central band. Title in MessinaModern Regular, a transitional serif, regular weight, dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height with the opening curly quote. Subtitle in MessinaModern Regular, serif, regular weight, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title in MessinaSans SemiBold, sans-serif, #545554, about 2.2% of slide height. Axis labels and footer text in MessinaSans Regular, sans-serif, #545454 or #535353, about 1.85% of slide height. Author name in MessinaSans Bold, #A6A6A6; date and page number in MessinaSans Book, #A6A6A6. Chart line and markers in red, about #E84A3C, with a line width of about 2 pt. Gridlines are thin dotted grey lines, about #A0A0A0. Flat design with no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

38

**Layout**

Two side-by-side stacked bar charts on a light grey page with a white middle band; the title is centred in a grey header band at the top, with the subtitle beneath it, both starting near x 20.5%. The legend sits at the far left (x about 5 to 17%) beside the USA chart, and the two charts occupy the centre and right of the white band, with the USA chart's left edge at x about 20.5% and the UK chart's at about 63%. The footer sits in a grey band at the bottom: source lines are left-aligned at x 20.5%, and the author, date and slide number are right-side, with the slide number at x 94.3% near the right margin.

**Visuals**

Two vertical stacked bar charts, both titled in sans-serif text above their plot areas: left 'Consumer generative AI chatbot use: USA' and right 'Consumer generative AI chatbot use: UK'. Each has a y-axis from 0% to 60% in 10% steps with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each 10% step, and a solid baseline at 0%; no axis title. Each has three categories on the x-axis: 2023, 2024, 2025. Series stacked bottom to top: Daily (red), Weekly (dark grey), Monthly (mid grey). No data labels are printed; values are estimates. USA: 2023 about Daily 2%, Weekly 1%, Monthly 13% (total about 16%); 2024 about Daily 4.6%, Weekly 4.1%, Monthly 26.9% (total about 35.6%); 2025 about Daily 10.2%, Weekly 7.6%, Monthly 33.2% (total about 51%). UK: 2023 about Daily 2%, Weekly 7%, Monthly 9% (total about 18%); 2024 about Daily 4%, Weekly 9%, Monthly 13% (total about 26%); 2025 about Daily 6%, Weekly 15%, Monthly 15% (total about 36%). Each segment has a thin white separator line at its top edge. Legend at left, top to bottom: grey square Monthly, dark grey square Weekly, red square Daily, with labels in sans-serif text. No highlighted points; the red Daily series is the only saturated colour on the slide.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 at top and bottom bands with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Light grey page (#F1F1F1) with a white central band; title and chart typography in a serif modern face for the headline and subtitle and a sans-serif for chart text. Title and subtitle text #545454, chart labels #545454 and #535353, footer text #545454 and footer author/date/number #A6A6A6. Accent colour is red #EA4B3F for the Daily series; series greys are #7F7F7F (Monthly) and #535353 (Weekly). Title is serif regular at about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle serif regular about 2.6%, chart titles sans-serif semibold about 2.2%, axis and legend labels sans-serif regular about 1.9%. Bars are flat rectangles with no corner radius, no shadows and no gradients; gridlines are thin dotted grey lines; the category baseline is a thin solid grey line.

### Slide 39

**Text**

Most data shows the same picture / Surveys are early, scattered and inconsistent, but an engagement gap seems clear / How many people use generative AI chatbots in the USA? / Weekly active / Daily active / 50% / 40% / 30% / 20% / 10% / 0% / Pew, Aug 2024 / CCIA, Nov 2024 / Blick et al RPS, Nov 2024 / Bain, Dec 2024 / Hartley et al, Dec 2024 / YouGov, Mar 2025 / Hartley et al, Jun 2025 / Reuters Inst., Jun 2025 / Deloitte, Jun 2025 / Bain, Sep 2025 / Source: Consumer surveys conducted as of date shown / Benedict Evans / –– November 2025 / 39

**Layout**

Full-width header band in light grey (#F1F1F1) holds the title and subtitle, left-aligned at about 20.5% from the left edge with the title top at about 5.6%. A white content band (20.5% to 86.1% of height) holds one bar chart with its chart title at 27.2% and the legend at the left (about 4.7% to 17%). A grey footer strip below the white band carries the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number at right, ending at about 95.5% from the left edge.

**Visuals**

Chart: stacked vertical bar chart, two series, titled 'How many people use generative AI chatbots in the USA?'. Y axis: percentage of people, 0% to 50%, gridlines every 10% (dotted, light grey). X axis: ten survey sources labelled with publisher and date, not a numeric axis. Series 1 'Weekly active' (dark grey #535353, stacked on top). Series 2 'Daily active' (red #EB483F, bottom of stack). Values (measured where the file gives a box, otherwise about): Pew Aug 2024 daily about 11%, weekly total about 26% (weekly segment about 15%); CCIA Nov 2024 daily about 13%, total about 29%; Blick et al RPS Nov 2024 daily about 9%, total about 38%; Bain Dec 2024 daily about 12%, total about 31%; Hartley et al Dec 2024 daily about 9%, total about 29%; YouGov Mar 2025 daily about 12%, total about 28%; Hartley et al Jun 2025 daily about 15%, total about 46%; Reuters Inst. Jun 2025 no daily segment, weekly total about 36%; Deloitte Jun 2025 daily about 10%, total about 18%; Bain Sep 2025 daily about 18%, total about 44%. Segments separated by thin white lines. Legend at upper left: dark grey square 'Weekly active' above red square 'Daily active'. No highlighted points.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from 20.5% to 86.1% of height. Light grey background #F1F1F1 for header and footer with white #FFFFFF content band. Title colour #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular serif, regular weight, about 5.6% of slide height (cap height about 3.9%). Subtitle #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular serif, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title in MessinaSans-SemiBold sans-serif #545554, about 2.2%. Axis and legend labels in MessinaSans-Regular sans-serif #545454 or #535353, about 1.85%. Footer: source line #545454 MessinaSans-Regular; 'Benedict Evans' #A6A6A6 MessinaSans-Bold; date and page number #A6A6A6 MessinaSans-Book, all about 1.85%. Accent colour red #EB483F; bars dark grey #535353. Bars are flat, square-cornered, no shadows or gradients. Gridlines dotted light grey, thin.

### Slide 40

**Text**

Is this just early? Or a harder problem?
Why do most users of ChatGPT only use it a little bit?
How many use cases are an obvious easy fit?
Who has a flexible job…
And consciously looks for ways to optimise?
For everyone else, do you need to wrap this in tooling and product?
Benedict Evans –– November 2025 40

**Layout**

Free-form composition on a single background: title top-left aligned to a left margin at about 20.5% of slide width, subtitle beneath it, and three equal circles in a horizontal row across the middle-lower area, separated by small right-pointing triangles. Footer sits bottom-right with author name, date and slide number on one baseline at about 91% of slide height; slide number is at the far right.

**Visuals**

Three filled circles connected left to right by two small grey right-pointing triangles (chevron-style arrows). Circle 1: dark grey #535353 with white serif text 'How many use cases are an obvious easy fit?'. Circle 2: dark grey #535353 with white serif text 'Who has a flexible job… And consciously looks for ways to optimise?'. Circle 3: red #EB483F with white serif text 'For everyone else, do you need to wrap this in tooling and product?'. Each circle is about 22.1% of slide width and 39.3% of slide height, flat with no shadow or outline, centred horizontally at evenly spaced positions (x 19.5%, 46.8%, 74.1%) and top at 33.9%. Triangles are light grey #A6A6A6 solid fills pointing right, placed between circles at about x 43%, and x 70.5%, vertically centred on the circles' midline at about 53%; each is roughly 2.2% of slide width wide and 6% tall. No photographs, logos or charts.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1, flat. Title and subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional serif, colour #545454; title about 5.6% of slide height (cap height roughly 3.9%), subtitle about 2.6%. Circle text in MessinaModern-Book serif, white #FFFFFF, about 3.3% of slide height, centred, line spacing about 1.2. Footer in MessinaSans, sans-serif: 'Benedict Evans' Bold and remainder Book, colour #A6A6A6, about 1.85% of slide height. Shapes are flat with no corner radius, shadow or gradient; circles are perfect circles. Accent colour is red #EB483F used only on the third circle.

none
