### Slide 42

**Text**

So where does a model lab compete?
Where is the value capture for a research-heavy, capital-intensive commodity?
Go down the stack
Win on scale?
(see: aircraft, AWS, chips)
If the models are commodities?
And don’t have network effects?
Go up the stack
Win on network effects & product?
(See: software)
Benedict Evans
–– November 2025
42

**Layout**

Three circles sit in one horizontal row across the centre of the slide, with a dark grey circle on the left, a dotted outline circle in the middle and a red circle on the right, each with its label centred inside. The title sits top-left at about x 20.5% with a one-line subtitle beneath it, both left-aligned on a shared left edge. The footer runs along the bottom right, with the author name, date and page number on one baseline near y 91%.

**Visuals**

Three circular shapes and two small arrowheads, no photographs or charts. Left: a solid dark grey (#535353) filled circle, about 22.1% wide and 39.3% tall, containing white text. Centre: a circle of the same size with a dotted dark grey (#535353) outline about 2 px thick and no fill, containing bold dark grey text. Right: a solid red (#EB483F) filled circle of the same size as the left one, containing white text. Between the left and centre circles is a solid light grey (#A6A6A6) triangle pointing left, about 2% wide and 7% tall, at about x 43.2%, y 49.8%. Between the centre and right circles is a matching light grey triangle pointing right, at about x 70.7%, y 49.8%. The arrowheads point outward from the centre circle, one toward the dark circle and one toward the red circle, and are not joined to the circles by lines.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Flat, minimal look on a single light grey background. Titles and centre text are dark grey #545454, the circle labels inside the solid circles are white, and the accent is the red circle #EB483F. Typeface is a Didone-like serif family, MessinaModern (Book and SemiBold weights), for titles and body. Footer uses MessinaSans in Bold and Book in light grey #A6A6A6. Title text is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, circle text about 3.3%, footer about 1.9%. Shapes have no corner radius, shadows or gradients, and the circles have flat fills with no outline.

OpenAI bundles and unbundles use cases / What’s the right experience? The right distribution? Here’s how ChatGPT Atlas and Google Gemini (inside Chrome) differ conceptually and practically: / 1. Core Architecture and Integration / Aspect / ChatGPT Atlas / Gemini in Chrome / Engine / A standalone browser built by OpenAI with ChatGPT natively inside every tab. / Chrome browser with Gemini available through the “Help me write” or “Ask Gemini” overlays. / AI Context / ChatGPT has deep contextual access to the page you’re on — it can read, summarize, act, and / Gemini operates mainly as a sidebar or overlay. It can see some page content but / Introducing ChatGPT / Ask anything / 11:30 / ChatGPT 5 / Booking.com find me a hotel in Paris for two adults between 11/21-11/24 with parking / Booking.com / Nov 21 - Nov 24 · 2 travelers / Zoku Paris / Fabulous · 2,493 reviews / 9.0 / Wifi Parking Pool Pets Res / Le Meurice – Dorch / Fabulous · 212 reviews / Wifi Parking Po / Benedict Evans –– November 2025 / 46

**Layout**

Centred title and subtitle sit across the top of the slide in one centred column, with the title at about 5.6% from the top and the subtitle beneath it. Three screenshots sit side by side in the lower two-thirds: a tall phone on the left, a desktop browser window in the centre (largest, slightly higher), and a tall phone on the right, with generous margins. The footer runs along the bottom right: author name, dash and date, then the slide number at the far right margin.

**Visuals**

Three device screenshots, none is a chart or table as a standalone graphic. Left: a photograph-like screen capture of a phone (Sora-style short-video app) showing a man in a yellow banana costume playing violin on top of a DJ mixing desk on a snowy mountain under a pale blue sky; he is centred, framed from knees to head with the phone's UI overlaid (For You header, avatar, like/comment/share icons, username tarekoyou and caption Welcome to Sora baby); dominant colours yellow, white, blue and black; the screen is rounded-rectangle masked with soft shadow. Centre: a macOS-style browser window with red, yellow and green traffic-light buttons, a tab titled Introducing ChatGPT, address bar showing openai.com and an Ask ChatGPT button; left pane shows the OpenAI Atlas announcement page with a black pill button Download for macOS and a white boxed area labelled Takes action for you; right pane shows a chat reply with a comparison table of columns Aspect, ChatGPT Atlas, Gemini in Chrome and rows Engine and AI Context, text partly cut off at the bottom edge of the pane. Right: a phone screenshot of the ChatGPT app with a user bubble asking for a Paris hotel on Booking.com, followed by Booking.com result cards: a photo of a bright hotel restaurant interior with pink and white chairs and plants (rating 9.0 badge in blue for Zoku Paris) and a photo of a terrace with cypress trees and a sunset sky (Le Meurice – Dorch..., cropped at the right edge of the phone). Colours throughout are neutral white and grey UI with black text, blue accents and the green OpenAI logo dot. No logos are placed outside the screenshots.

**Layout**

Single full-width content slide with a grey #F1F1F1 header band holding a left-aligned title and subtitle starting at about 20.5% from the left, a white middle band (y 20.5% to 86.1%) holding a stacked column chart with a legend at the far left (x 4.8%) and the chart plot spanning about x 17% to 95%, and a grey #F1F1F1 footer band with the source line at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and slide number right-aligned at the bottom right, ending at about x 95.6%.

**Visuals**

One stacked column chart. Type: vertical stacked column chart, about 23 quarterly columns, each split into a grey Government segment at the bottom and a red Enterprise segment on top. Chart title printed above the plot: 'Palantir quarterly revenue by segment ($m)'. Y axis: labelled 0, 250, 500, 750, 1,000, 1,250 (units $m), range 0 to about 1,250 with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled value; the plot top sits just above 1,250. X axis: category labels Mar 2020, Mar 2021, Mar 2022, Mar 2023, Mar 2024, Mar 2025, one label every fourth column, with a thin grey baseline at 0. Legend at upper left of the chart: red square labelled Enterprise above a dark grey square labelled Government. Series values are not printed; the following are estimates marked about, given per column in order as Government / Enterprise total: column 1 about 120 / 230; column 2 about 140 / 250; column 3 about 160 / 290; column 4 about 190 / 320; column 5 about 210 / 340; column 6 about 230 / 375; column 7 about 215 / 390; column 8 about 240 / 430; column 9 about 240 / 445; column 10 about 260 / 475; column 11 about 270 / 475; column 12 about 290 / 505; column 13 about 285 / 520; column 14 about 300 / 530; column 15 about 305 / 560; column 16 about 320 / 610; column 17 about 330 / 630; column 18 about 370 / 680; column 19 about 410 / 720; column 20 about 455 / 825; column 21 about 490 / 885; column 22 about 555 / 1,000; column 23 about 635 / 1,180. No highlighted points. Columns have thin white separator lines between segments and are flat-topped with no shadows; the Mar 2020 column sits at the left edge of the plot and the last column at the right edge.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-slide background with a white #FFFFFF band across the middle. Minimal editorial style on a light grey and white two-band layout. Title in a high-contrast serif (MessinaModern-Regular, close to a Modern/Didone serif), regular weight, #545454, title size about 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, regular, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and all chart and footer text in a geometric grotesque sans (MessinaSans family): chart title SemiBold #545554 about 2.2%; axis and legend labels Regular #545454 or #535353 about 1.85%; author name Bold #A6A6A6 and date and slide number Book #A6A6A6 about 1.85%. Enterprise segments red #EB4B3C; Government segments dark grey #505050. Gridlines light grey dotted, about 0.5 px. Background bands #F1F1F1 with a white #FFFFFF middle band. No rounded corners, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 55

**Text**

Pilots come first and deployment takes time / “Agentic!’ is 2025’s buzzword, but deployment takes longer / AI ‘agent’ use by business function, where Generative AI is already used. June 2025 / Experiment / 30% / Pilot / Deploying / Deployed / 20% / 10% / 0% / IT / Knowledge / Marketing / Service / Product / Software / HR / Supply / Manufacturing / management / development / chain / Source: McKinsey / Benedict Evans / –– November 2025 / 55

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey slide with a grey title band across the top (about the top 20% of the height) holding a left-aligned serif title and subtitle starting at about 20.5% from the left. A white panel fills the middle band (about 20.5% to 86% of the height), with the chart subtitle and a left-side legend at about 4.8% from the left, and the bar chart spanning about 17% to 95% of the width. The bottom grey band holds the left-aligned source line at about 20.5% from the left and a right-aligned footer 'Benedict Evans –– November 2025' with page number 55 at the far right, roughly 94% across.

**Visuals**

Stacked vertical bar chart, titled 'AI ‘agent’ use by business function, where Generative AI is already used. June 2025'. Y-axis: percentage of respondents, 0% to 30% in 10% steps, with faint dotted gridlines at 10%, 20% and 30%. X-axis: nine business functions (IT, Knowledge management, Marketing, Service, Product, Software development, HR, Supply chain, Manufacturing), no axis unit label. Four stacked series from bottom to top: Deployed (red #E94B3C), Deploying (light pink #F9DAD9), Pilot (dark grey #545454), Experiment (mid grey #A6A6A6). Values are not printed; estimates: IT about 2 deployed, 8 deploying, 6 pilot, 7 experiment (total about 23); Knowledge management about 1, 7, 6, 12 (total about 26); Marketing about 1, 6, 6, 11 (total about 24); Service about 1, 6, 7, 8 (total about 22); Product about 1, 5, 6, 9 (total about 21); Software development about 1, 5, 5, 6 (total about 17); HR about 0.5, 3, 3, 5 (total about 11.5); Supply chain about 0.5, 2, 3, 4 (total about 9.5); Manufacturing about 0.5, 1, 1, 3 (total about 5.5). Legend at upper left listing Experiment, Pilot, Deploying, Deployed as square swatches from top to bottom. No highlighted points. Bars are about 3.3% of slide width with thin white separators between segments.

**Design**

### Slide 56

**Text**

Not everything works? Welcome to tech
“Why did our AI pilot fail?” That’s a CTO question, not an AI question
Security, / privacy, IPR, / error rates, / legal
Data / integration / & legacy / systems
Finding the / right solution / for the right / people
The same / issues as / deploying any / new tech
Benedict Evans –– November 2025 56

**Layout**

Three-quarter-width content block starting at x 20 with a title and subtitle left-aligned at the same margin, above a horizontal row of four equal circles evenly spaced across the centre of the slide. Footer line sits bottom-right with author name, date and slide number on one baseline; no logo, no left-side content, and the left fifth of the slide is empty.

**Visuals**

Four solid circles in a single horizontal row, equal size (about 16.2% of slide width, 28.8% of slide height, i.e. circles), spaced at 20% intervals, vertically centred around 53% of slide height. Circles 1 to 3 are dark grey #535353 with white serif text; circle 4 is red #EB483F with white serif text, marking the contrast point. Each circle holds a short centred label: 'Security, privacy, IPR, error rates, legal'; 'Data integration & legacy systems'; 'Finding the right solution for the right people'; 'The same issues as deploying any new tech'. No photographs, icons, logos, or charts.

Background: Flat light grey #F1F1F1 covering the full slide.

### Slide 57

**Text**

The future can take time / A quarter of CIOs have launched something - but 40% don’t plan anything until at least 2026 / CIO expected timing for first LLM projects in production, September 2025 / Already deployed / at least one project / H2 2025 / 2026 / Later or no plan / 0% / 25% / 50% / 75% / 100% / Source: Morgan Stanley CIO Survey / Benedict Evans / –– November 2025 / 57

**Layout**

Single-column slide with a full-width light grey header band (top 20.5%) holding a left-aligned title and subtitle, a white central band containing a 100% stacked horizontal bar chart with its title above, and a light grey footer band (bottom 14%) holding the source line at left and the author, date and slide number at right. The title starts at x 20.5% and all left-aligned content shares that left edge; the right edge of the chart and footer is at about 95.3%.

**Visuals**

One chart: a single horizontal 100% stacked bar. Chart title: CIO expected timing for first LLM projects in production, September 2025. Horizontal axis runs 0% to 100% with tick labels 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%, marked by faint dotted vertical gridlines at 25%, 50%, 75% and 100% plus a solid thin grey vertical axis line at 0%. No vertical axis labels. Segments left to right: Already deployed at least one project (red #EB483F, about 33% of the bar); H2 2025 (pale pink #FBDAD9, about 25%); 2026 (black #000000, about 21%); Later or no plan (dark grey #535353, about 21%). The slide prints no numeric values for the segments, so the widths are estimates. No legend; segments are labelled inside. No highlighted points. Segments are separated by thin white gaps.

**Design**

Background is light grey #F1F1F1 in the top and bottom bands, with a white #FFFFFF central band spanning about 20.5% to 86.1% of slide height. Title colour #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular serif, regular weight, about 5.56% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, about 2.59% of slide height, colour #545454. Chart title in MessinaSans-SemiBold sans-serif, about 2.22% of slide height, colour #545554. Bar labels and axis labels in MessinaSans-Regular sans-serif about 1.85% of slide height. Body and source text #545454. Footer author name MessinaSans-Bold and date/slide number MessinaSans-Book in #A6A6A6. Accent colours: red #EB483F, pale pink #FBDAD9, black #000000, dark grey #535353. Shapes are sharp-cornered rectangles with no shadows or gradients; gridlines are dotted light grey.

58

**Layout**

Full-width slide split into three horizontal bands: a light grey header band (top 0 to 20.5%) holding the title and subtitle, a white content band (20.5% to 86.1%) holding the line chart, and a light grey footer band (86.1% to 100%) holding the source line at left and the author, date and slide number at right. The title, subtitle and chart title share a left edge at x 20.5%; the chart legend sits at the left margin at x 4.8%. The footer source line is at x 20.5%, and the slide number ends at about x 95.5%, with a right margin of about 4.5%.

**Visuals**

One line chart, titled 'Enterprise workloads in public cloud', plotting two series against a year axis from 2013 to 2025 and a percentage axis from 0% to 60%. The y axis is labelled in percent with 10% gridlines, drawn as light grey dotted horizontal lines at 10%, 20%, 30%, 40%, 50% and 60%, and a solid grey baseline at 0%. The x axis shows the years 2013 to 2025 as labels, with a thin grey baseline. Both series are smoothed curves with circular markers at each survey point; no values are printed on the chart, so the values below are estimates marked about. Series 'Today' (red line and red markers, about #EE4B3A): about 4% in 2014, about 5% at 2014.6, about 6% in 2015, about 11% at 2015.5, about 18% in 2016, about 20% at 2016.5, about 19% in 2017, about 18% at 2017.5, about 26% in 2018, about 19% at 2018.5, about 23% in 2019, about 23% at 2019.5, about 22% in 2020, about 21% at 2020.5, about 22% in 2021, about 24% at 2021.5, about 31% in 2022, about 29% in 2023, about 29.5% at 2023.5, about 28% in 2024 and about 28% at 2024.5. Series 'Expected in 3 years' (dark grey line and markers, about #545454): about 9% in 2014, about 14% at 2014.6, about 16% in 2015, about 19% at 2015.5, about 38% in 2016, about 33% at 2016.5, about 34.5% in 2017, about 34% at 2017.5, about 38% in 2018, about 36% at 2018.5, about 43% in 2019, about 42% at 2019.5, about 41% in 2020, about 40% at 2020.5, about 41.5% in 2021, about 42% at 2021.5, about 51% in 2022, about 47% in 2023, about 47.5% at 2023.5, about 46% in 2024 and about 45.5% at 2024.5. Legend at upper left of the chart, outside the plot, listing 'Today' with a red dot marker above 'Expected in 3 years' with a dark grey dot marker. No points are highlighted. The gap between the series is widest in 2016 and 2022. Chart background is white with no border.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 across the full slide, with a white #FFFFFF band behind the chart from y 20.5% to 86.1%. Minimal, flat editorial style on a light grey and white base. Title in a serif face (MessinaModern-Regular, close to a transitional serif such as Georgia) in dark grey #545454, regular weight, roughly 5.6% of slide height. Subtitle in the same serif, dark grey #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and all chart and footer text in a humanist sans (MessinaSans family): chart title SemiBold #545554 at about 2.2%; axis labels, legend and source line Regular at about 1.85%, colored #535353 to #545454; footer author name Bold and date and page number Book in light grey #A6A6A6 at about 1.85%. Accent colour is red #EE4B3A for the 'Today' series; the other series is dark grey #545454. Lines are about 2 to 3 px wide with round markers about 1.5% of slide width in diameter. Gridlines are thin dotted light grey, no shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

**Layout**

Full-bleed light-grey slide split into a grey header band, a white chart band and a grey footer band. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at about x 20.5% in the header, the chart title sits above a line chart spanning x 16% to 95%, and the footer holds a source line at left and author, date and page number at right, all on a common left margin near x 20.5%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'Average SKUs per supermarket, USA'. Y-axis: number of SKUs, range 0 to 60,000, gridlines every 10,000 (dotted light grey). X-axis: years, 1950 to 2020 labelled every 10 years, axis line solid grey. Single series drawn as a smoothed red line (about #E8473A) with no markers: starts about 3,000 in 1955, about 6,000 in 1960, about 8,500 in 1974, about 14,500 in 1980, about 26,000 in 1990, about 32,000 in 2000, about 35,000 in 2003, peak about 51,000 in 2008, about 47,000 in 2010, about 39,000 in 2016, about 31,000 in 2020, about 32,000 in 2024. No printed data values; figures are estimates marked 'about'. A vertical dashed dark grey line (about #545454, roughly 2 px dotted) at 1974 rises from the x-axis to just below the 60,000 gridline, annotated 'Grocery barcodes launched in 1974' to its right. No legend. Highlighted point: the 1974 dashed marker; the 2008 peak is not marked.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF band from y 20.5% to 86% behind the chart. Light grey #F1F1F1 background with a white #FFFFFF chart band. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, about 5.6% of slide height, colour #545454. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, about 2.6% of height, #545454. Chart title and axis labels in MessinaSans sans-serif, about 1.9% to 2.2% of height, grey #545554. Accent red line #E8473A, 2 to 3 px stroke. Dotted gridlines light grey. Footer text in MessinaSans, about 1.9%, #A6A6A6 with bold author name. Flat, no shadows, no gradients, square corners.

### Slide 62

**Text**

Where might we look for change?
What can LLM automation unbundle? What things did we not realise were bundles?
Online distribution
unbundled physical
assets
What do LLMs
unbundle?
Internet
unbundling created
new aggregators
How do LLMs do
that better?
Benedict Evans
–– November 2025
62

**Layout**

Centred composition on a plain light-grey field. The title sits top-left at about x 20.5%, y 5.6%, with a one-line subtitle directly beneath it at y 13.3%. Two equal circles sit side by side in the vertical middle of the slide, separated by a dotted vertical red rule centred at about x 53%. Footer runs along the bottom right at y 91%: bold author name, en-dash, date, then the page number at x 94.3%.

**Visuals**

Two solid filled circles of equal size, each about 22.1% of slide width and 39.3% of slide height. The left circle is dark grey #535353 and holds white serif text in two paragraphs: 'Online distribution unbundled physical assets' then 'What do LLMs unbundle?'. The right circle is red #EB483F and holds white serif text in two paragraphs: 'Internet unbundling created new aggregators' then 'How do LLMs do that better?'. Between them is a vertical dotted line in red #EB483F, about 0.2% of slide width, running from about y 31.6% to y 78%, with round-looking dots. No photographs, charts, logos or icons.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1, flat with no pattern or photograph. Flat, minimal look with no gradients, shadows or corner treatments. Background is light grey #F1F1F1. Title is a large serif in grey #545454, subtitle is a smaller serif in the same grey. Circle text is white serif book weight. Accent colour is red #EB483F, used for the right circle and the dotted divider. Dark grey #535353 is used for the left circle. Typefaces are MessinaModern (Regular for titles, Book for circle text), a modern serif. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, subtitle about 2.6%, circle text about 3.0%, footer sans about 1.85% (MessinaSans Bold for the name, Book for the rest, colour #A6A6A6).

### Slide 64

**Text**

How do we use automation that makes ‘mistakes’?
We have no indication that error rates will go away, so where’s the human in the loop?
Do ‘errors’ matter?
Can you automate verification?
Is human verification efficient?
How much do we need to wrap the LLM in software?
Benedict Evans –– November 2025 64

**Layout**

Four circles in a single horizontal row across the middle-lower part of the slide, joined by three grey arrowheads, with a left-aligned serif title and subtitle at the top starting at about 20.5% from the left margin. A footer line sits at the bottom right with the author name, date and slide number.

Four circular flow nodes connected left to right by three small grey right-pointing triangular arrowheads. Each circle is about 16.2% of slide width and 28.8% of slide height, with white serif text centred inside. The arrowheads are light grey (about #A6A6A6), roughly 3.5% of slide width, sitting in the gaps between circles, pointing right. No photographs, charts or logos.

### Slide 65

**Text**

What do ‘automated interns’ change?
The Jevons paradox - applied price elasticity
Do you do the
same work with
fewer people?
Or more work
with the same
people?
Was employing
lots of people
your moat?
What becomes
possible when
you don’t need
millions of people
to do that?
Benedict Evans
–– November 2025

**Layout**

Full-bleed slide with a light grey (#F1F1F1) header band across the top about 20% of the height, holding a left-aligned serif title and subtitle starting at about x 20.5%. A white content panel spans the full width from about y 20.5% to 86%, containing a single clustered column chart with its legend at the far left (x about 3%) and the plot area from about x 20.5% to 95%. A grey footer band sits below the white panel; the source line is at left (x 20.5%) and the author, date and page number are right-aligned at the bottom right, with the page number at about x 94% and the title, subtitle and chart title all sharing the left edge at about x 20.5%.

**Visuals**

One clustered column chart. Chart title: 'UK population and steam engine labour unit equivalent (m)'. Y axis: unlabelled axis title, values in millions (m), range 0 to 300 with gridlines and labels every 50 (0, 50, 100, 150, 200, 250, 300); the gridlines are light grey dotted lines. X axis: categories 1840, 1870, 1896, with a solid mid-grey baseline at 0. Series 1 'Population' in dark grey #545454, about 16 (1840), about 25 (1870), about 34 (1896), no printed data labels. Series 2 'Steam engine equivalent' in red #EB483F, about 11 (1840), about 79 (1870), about 273 (1896), no printed data labels. Legend at top-left of chart: dark grey square with 'Population' above a red square with 'Steam engine equivalent', both set in sans-serif about 1.85% of slide height. No highlighted points. Bars are flat, with no outlines, and the two bars in each pair sit side by side with a small white gap.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide, with a white #FFFFFF panel from about y 20.5% to 86%. Light grey #F1F1F1 header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF central panel. Chart title in dark grey #545554 semibold sans-serif (MessinaSans-SemiBold), about 2.2%. Axis and legend labels in #535353 or #545454 regular sans-serif (MessinaSans-Regular) about 1.85%. Footer: 'Benedict Evans' in bold #A6A6A6, date in book weight #A6A6A6, source line in regular #545454. Accent colour is red #EB483F for the steam series only. ### Slide 67

**Text**

Where’s the human in the loop?
All recommendation systems today work by driving, capturing and analysing user activity
Pre-internet
Human editors, for both retail & media
Physical assets as moat
Internet
All of us are mechanical turks feeding algorithms
Network effects as moat
GenAI?
Can an LLM do this better?
Can an LLM do it without needing a user base?
Benedict Evans –– November 2025 67

**Layout**

Three equal white circles sit in a single horizontal row across the middle of the slide, centred vertically in the content area, with two small grey arrow triangles between them. The title is left-aligned at about x 20.5% near the top, with a one-line subtitle directly beneath it, both sharing the same left edge. The footer line sits bottom-right at about y 91%, with the author name in bold, then the date, then the page number, right-aligned to about x 95.5%.

**Visuals**

Three white circles with thin dark grey outlines, each about 21.7% of slide width and 38.5% of slide height, arranged left to right with even spacing. The first two circles have dark grey outlines (#545454, about 0.3% of slide width). The third circle has a red outline (about #E8473F) of the same weight. Between circle 1 and circle 2 is a solid grey right-pointing triangle (#A6A6A6) with its point to the right, about 1.9% wide and 6.8% tall, centred vertically on the circles. Between circle 2 and circle 3 is an identical grey triangle. Circle 1 contains the heading Pre-internet with two body items. Circle 2 contains the heading Internet with two body items. Circle 3 contains the heading GenAI? with two body questions. Diagram: three circles connected in sequence by arrows, flowing left to right, labelled Pre-internet, Internet, GenAI?.

**Design**

Background: Solid flat light grey #F1F1F1. Flat light grey background (#F1F1F1) with no gradient, texture or imagery. Titles and body text are dark grey #545454 in MessinaModern, a transitional serif with Regular, Book and SemiBold weights. The title is Regular at about 5.6% of slide height, the subtitle Regular at about 2.6%, circle headings SemiBold at about 3.3%, and body lines Regular or Book at about 3.0%. Footer is MessinaSans in Bold (author) and Book (date and number) at about 1.9%, colour #A6A6A6. Circles are white (#FFFFFF) filled with stroke about 0.3% of slide width, no shadow, no corner radius needed as they are true circles. Arrows are flat #A6A6A6 with no outline. Only the GenAI circle uses the red accent outline. No shadows or gradients anywhere.

### Slide 68

**Text**

Value to capture!
Brands spend a trillion dollars a year to talk to consumers - plus rent, shipping, marketing, returns…
Global ad revenue, 2024 ($bn)
Google Search
Google Network
YouTube
Meta
Amazon
All other ex-China
China
0
100
200
300
400
500
600
700
800
900
1,000
1,100
Source: Companies, WPP Media
NB: ‘China’ represents spending in China, not global spending by Chinese companies
Benedict Evans
–– November 2025
68

**Layout**

Full-width horizontal layout on a light grey (#F1F1F1) slide with a grey header band across the top (0 to 20.5% of height) and a grey footer band at the bottom (below about 86%); the white chart area sits between them. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at x 20.5%, with the chart heading below them at the same left edge. A single horizontal stacked bar spans the chart width at mid-height, with a dotted-gridline x-axis scale from 0 to 1,100 beneath it. Source and note lines sit bottom-left at x 20.5%; the author name, date and page number sit bottom-right, the page number at x 94.3%.

**Visuals**

Chart: a single horizontal 100% stacked-length bar (stacked segments in one row), titled 'Global ad revenue, 2024 ($bn)'. X-axis: linear, unit $bn, range 0 to 1,100, labelled ticks at 0, 100, 200, 300, 400, 500, 600, 700, 800, 900, 1,000, 1,100, with thin dotted vertical gridlines at each tick. No y-axis; a thin grey vertical baseline at x 0. Segments left to right, each labelled on the bar: Google Search (about 0 to 200, red #EB483F, label horizontal), Google Network (about 200 to 230, red #EB483F, label rotated vertical), YouTube (about 230 to 265, red #EB483F, label rotated vertical), Meta (about 265 to 425, salmon #F0766F, label horizontal), Amazon (about 425 to 483, pale pink #F8DBD8, label rotated vertical, dark text #535353), All other ex-China (about 483 to 860, dark grey #535353, label horizontal), China (about 860 to 1,035, mid grey #A7A7A7, label horizontal). No segment has a printed numeric value, so all boundaries are estimates marked 'about'. White 1 px separators between segments. No legend; labels are on the segments. No highlighted points beyond colour contrast of the red segments (Google-owned group) against the grey ones.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 full-slide fill, with a white #FFFFFF band across the middle (20.5% to 86% of height). Light grey #F1F1F1 background for header and footer bands, with a white #FFFFFF chart panel between them. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, large (about 5.6% of slide height). Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, about 2.6% of height. Chart heading and axis labels in MessinaSans sans-serif, semibold for heading (#545554) and regular for ticks (#545554). Segment labels in MessinaSans-Regular, white on the red, salmon and dark grey segments, #535353 on the pale pink segment, about 1.85% of height. Footer in MessinaSans-Regular #545454 for source and note, #A6A6A6 for author, date and page number (author in Bold, date in Book). Accent colours: red #EB483F, salmon #F0766F, pale pink #F8DBD8, dark grey #535353, mid grey #A7A7A7. Gridlines are thin dotted grey. No shadows, gradients or rounded corners.

L'ORÉAL GROUPE
Augmenting creativity with Generative AI
CreAltech is revolutionizing beauty content creation at L'Oréal, augmenting the creativity of our marketing and content teams with the power of Generative AI. Combining a modular ecosystem of models, expertise, technologies, and partners - including Google, Adobe and OMI- CreAltech empowers marketers to generate thousands of unique, on-brand images, texts, and videos for diverse platforms and global audiences.
As L'Oreal Groupe, we have decided that we will not use AI Generated 'lifelike' images of human face, body, hair or skin, to support/ enhance product benefits, in our external communication. CreAltech prioritizes responsible AI asset production with a robust ethical framework, focusing on commercially safe AI-generated materials for core activities:
Early Ideation & Prototyping: Generating initial concepts, creating and quickly developing storyboards. • Visual Innovation: Inspiring visual codes of beauty and pushing creative boundaries.
Scaled Production: Efficiently creating a high volume of content: ingredients, textures, formulae, backgrounds, and product shots leveraging commercially safe models. 72

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) slide split into three horizontal bands: a grey header band across the top about 20% of the height, a white middle band holding the chart, and a grey footer band below. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at about 20.5% from the left edge, on the same left edge as the chart title, the y-axis and the 'Source: Bain' line. The legend sits in a narrow column at the far left (about 3% from the left edge), outside the content column. The chart fills the white band from about 20% to 95% of the width, with the x-axis category labels below the baseline. The footer line 'Benedict Evans –– November 2025' is right-aligned, with the page number 72 at the far right margin (about 94% to 96%) at about 91% from the top.

**Visuals**

Chart: 100% stacked column chart, six columns, with the title 'US consumer search preference (September 2025)' above the plot. Y-axis: 0% to 100%, labelled at 0%, 25%, 50%, 75%, 100%, with dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled value and a solid grey baseline at 0%; no unit beyond the percent sign. X-axis: categories All, Under 30, 30-45, 45-60, 60-80, Older (no axis title). Segments stacked bottom to top: Always GenAI (red), Mostly GenAI (light pink), Mixed (mid grey), Mostly seach (dark grey), Always search (black). The slide prints no values, so all segment values are estimates marked about: All: Always GenAI about 5%, Mostly GenAI about 11%, Mixed about 28%, Mostly seach about 26%, Always search about 30%. Under 30: Always GenAI about 8%, Mostly GenAI about 17%, Mixed about 33%, Mostly seach about 24%, Always search about 18%. 30-45: Always GenAI about 8%, Mostly GenAI about 16%, Mixed about 34%, Mostly seach about 24%, Always search about 18%. 45-60: Always GenAI about 5%, Mostly GenAI about 9%, Mixed about 31%, Mostly seach about 28%, Always search about 27%. 60-80: Always GenAI about 2%, Mostly GenAI about 5%, Mixed about 18%, Mostly seach about 27%, Always search about 48%. Older: Always GenAI about 0%, Mostly GenAI about 0%, Mixed about 14%, Mostly seach about 18%, Always search about 68%. Legend: vertical list at the left, five square swatches with labels in the order Always search, Mostly seach, Mixed, Mostly GenAI, Always GenAI. No highlighted points. Flat fills, no gradients, no photographs, logos or icons. Thin white separators (about 0.1% of height) between stacked segments.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1, with a white #FFFFFF band across the middle of the slide. Light grey (#F1F1F1) header and footer bands with a white (#FFFFFF) chart band. Titles and subtitle in serif (MessinaModern-Regular, regular weight) in dark grey #545454. Chart title in sans-serif (MessinaSans-SemiBold) #545554. ### Slide 73

**Text**

The web has been dying since 1997
Remember how early this is, and how hard it is to know how the new thing will work
We interrupt this magazine / for a special bulletin – / PUSH! / Kiss your browser goodbye: / The radical future of / media beyond the Web / By the editors of Wired / Remember the browser war / between Netscape and Microsoft? / Well, forget it. The Web browser / itself is about to croak. And / good riddance. In its place ... / Umberto Eco / Confab Clips / Copyright Cartel / Po Bronson on / Building the VW PC / The Cisco Mantra: / :-) :-) :-) / Sambung! / $4.95 / Canada 5.95 / THE NEW WAY TO WATCH TV p.136 / THE COOLEST GADGET PROTOTYPES OF ALL TIME! p.128 / THE MOST TOXIC TOWN IN AMERICA p.162 / WIRED / The / Web is / dead. / WHAT HAPPENED / AND WHY / BY CHRIS ANDERSON / BY MICHAEL WOLFF / TECHNOLOGY / Online news publishers face 'extinction-level event' from Google's AI-powered search / JULY 31, 2025 · 4:20 AM ET / HEARD ON MORNING EDITION / By Bobby Allyn, John Ruwitch / When Google unveiled AI Overviews last year, online publishers worried that the AI-generated blurbs in the top spot of search results would spark precipitous declines in traffic and gut the business model of vast reaches of the web. / Now, there is growing evidence validating those fears. / New research shows the web traffic that publishers have long relied on is significantly slowing, thanks to AI-generated summaries and the rise of AI chatbots. / Traffic to CNN's website has dropped about 30% from a year ago. Business Insider's and HuffPost's sites saw traffic plunges around 40% in the same period, according to figures from digital market data firm Similarweb. / npr.org / DONATE / Source: Zeldman / Benedict Evans –– November 2025 73

**Layout**

Centred title and subtitle block across the top, with the title starting at about x 20.5% and the subtitle below it. Three visuals sit in a row across the middle: a stacked pair of magazine covers on the left overlapping each other, an arrow between them and a browser window on the right. A source line sits bottom left at about x 20.5%, and the footer with author, date and slide number sits bottom right ending at about x 95.6%.

**Visuals**

Left: a photographed magazine cover (Wired-style parody, 1280 by 800 pixels in the file, partly hidden) showing a large cyan hand on a red and yellow background with yellow headline text PUSH! and cover lines in white, yellow and black, with a barcode bottom left. Overlapping it in front: a Wired cover (1105 by 1500 pixels) on a red-orange #FF3B0F field with black condensed masthead letters WIRED in white boxes, large black headline 'The Web is dead.', and a cream bar at its lower left. Centre: a solid grey right-pointing triangle arrow, about #A0A0A0, pointing from the covers to the browser window. Right: a screenshot of a browser window (1434 by 1680 pixels) with three traffic-light dots (red, yellow, green), an address bar reading npr.org, an NPR logo of red n, black p and blue r, and a red DONATE button with a heart, showing a news article with a rounded window frame and soft drop shadow.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1 covering the whole slide. Light grey background #F1F1F1. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, colour #545454, about 5.56% of slide height. Subtitle in MessinaModern-Regular serif, #545454, about 2.59%. Footer in MessinaSans, #A6A6A6, about 1.85%; author name bold, date and slide number book weight. Source line in MessinaSans-Regular #545454, about 1.85%. Images have soft drop shadows. Browser window has rounded corners and a soft shadow. Article text in the screenshot is a serif body with headline in bold sans.

### Slide 74

**Text**

But where are we going?
What if recommendations go from correlation to an understanding of what those SKUs really represent?
You bought
packing tape -
maybe you
need boxes and
bubblewrap?
Maybe you’re
moving. How
about some
lightbulbs and
smoke alarms?
Here’s an ad for
home insurance
Benedict Evans
–– November 2025
74

**Layout**

Two-line header block sits top-left, with the title left-aligned at x 20.5% and the subtitle aligned to the same left edge beneath it. How about some lightbulbs and smoke alarms?'. Circle 3 is red #EB483F and contains 'Here’s an ad for home insurance'. The arrowheads are about 3.9% wide and 6.8% tall, filled #A6A6A6, sitting between the circles at about y 49.8% to 56.6%. No photographs, charts or logos.

### Slide 75

**Text**

How do we know what we / might want? / For 30 years we’ve had infinite product, infinite / media and infinite retail / Now we have a machine that sees all of it, and / sees us / What does it recommend? / Source: Morioka Shoten / Benedict Evans / –– November 2025 / 75

**Layout**

Two-column split layout: the left half holds the text on a light grey field, with the title at top-left and the three-paragraph body beneath it, left-aligned at a 4.6% left margin. The right half is a full-bleed photograph. A source line sits at the bottom left and a footer row (author, date, slide number) runs along the bottom of the left column and right edge.

**Visuals**

One photograph occupying the right half of the slide, full-bleed to the top, right and bottom edges. Subject: the street-level entrance of a small gallery seen through a black-framed glass door, showing the interior: white walls with framed small prints hung in a row, a tall wooden cabinet with many small drawers against the back wall, a wooden display plinth with a framed artwork and a small glass vitrine on a black metal stand in the foreground, a pendant lamp, a small wooden stool, and a sign reading 絵本『森のはずれの美術館の話』刊行記念 / ゲオルグ・ハレンスレーベン展 with a date line beneath. Action: none, a static interior view. Composition: frontal, centred on the open glass door, with the doorway frame filling the top and sides of the frame and the concrete pavement at the bottom. Lighting: warm, low-level interior light against a dark exterior. Dominant colours: warm cream and ochre wood, black frame, pale grey concrete floor. The photo is cropped to fill the box with no border, shadow or overlay.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F2F2F2 across the full slide, with white #FFFFFF behind the right half under the photograph. Light grey background (#F2F2F2) with a white (#FFFFFF) panel behind the photo area. Title and body text are dark grey (#545454) in a Messina Modern serif, regular weight; the footer author name and date and page number are light grey (#A6A6A6) in a Messina Sans, with the author in bold; the source line is dark grey (#545454) in Messina Sans Book. Title is about 5.6% of slide height, body about 3.0%, footer and source about 1.9%. No accent colour, no rules, no shapes other than the photo, no corner radii, shadows or gradients.

### Slide 76

**Text**

**Layout**

Three-part horizontal diagram of equal-sized circles on a single row, centred vertically in the middle band of the slide. The title sits top-left at x 20.5% and the subtitle is left-aligned beneath it at the same x. A dark circle sits left, a dotted outline circle sits centre, and a red circle sits right, with a small grey triangle between each pair. The footer runs along the bottom right at y 91% with the page number at the far right edge.

**Visuals**

Diagram with three circles and two triangles. Left: a solid filled circle, dark grey #535353, containing the text 'Logistics, data utility, answers' and 'Solve my problem', centred. Centre: a circle with no fill and a dotted outline in dark grey #535353, about 2 px stroke, containing the bold text 'Why did you ask for that?' and 'Do you care where it comes from?', centred. Right: a solid filled circle, red #EB483F, containing 'Experience, curation, enjoyment, authenticity', centred. Between the left circle and the centre circle is a left-pointing solid triangle in light grey #A6A6A6. Between the centre circle and the right circle is a right-pointing solid triangle in light grey #A6A6A6. The triangles point outward from the centre circle, toward the two filled circles. No arrows connect the circles beyond these triangles, and no photographs, logos or icons appear.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F1F1F1. Plain light grey background #F1F1F1 with no gradient or texture. Title and subtitle are dark grey #545454 in MessinaModern-Regular, a serif with book-weight letterforms; the title is about 5.6% of slide height and the subtitle about 2.6%. Circle body text is 3.3% of slide height, white #FFFFFF in MessinaModern-Book for the filled circles, and dark grey #545454 in MessinaModern-SemiBold for the centre circle. Footer is MessinaSans in light grey #A6A6A6, with the name in Bold and the date and page number in Book, about 1.85% of slide height. Shapes are flat with no shadows, no rounded corners beyond the true circles, and no line on the filled circles.

### Slide 79

**Text**

The old stuff from before ChatGPT is still here
Excluding gas and grocery, 30% of US retail sales are now online
E-commerce as % US retail sales
Core retail
Addressable retail
All retail
35%
30%
25%
20%
15%
10%
5%
0%
2000
2005
2010
2015

Source: US Census. Seasonally adjusted
Benedict Evans
–– November 2025
79
‘Retail’ excludes restaurants & bars. ‘Addressable retail’ also excludes cars, car parts & service,
and gasoline stations. ‘Core retail’ excludes grocery from addressable retail.

**Layout**

Full-bleed light grey (#F1F1F1) band across the top and bottom with a white band between them spanning about y 20.5% to 86.1%. The title sits top-left-of-centre at x 20.5%, with the subtitle directly beneath it aligned to the same left edge. The chart occupies the white band from x 5% to about x 96%, with a legend at the far left and the chart title above the plot. The footer source note is left-aligned at x 20.5% in the bottom band, with the author, date and page number right-aligned on the same baseline row at about y 91%.

**Visuals**

Line chart titled 'E-commerce as % US retail sales'. Y axis: percent of US retail sales, range 0% to 35%, labelled every 5% (0%, 5%, 10%, 15%, 20%, 25%, 30%, 35%), with light dotted horizontal gridlines at each labelled value. X axis: years 2000 to 2025, labelled at 2000, 2005, 2010, 2015, 2020 and 2025, with a solid grey baseline at 0%. Three series, all solid lines about 0.3% of slide height thick with rounded joins. Core retail (red #E8473B line): about 1.7% in 2000, about 7% in 2008, a sharp spike to about 29.6% in early 2020, dipping to about 26.8% in mid-2020, about 26.5% in 2022, and ending about 29% in 2025. Addressable retail (dark grey #4A4A4A line): about 1.2% in 2000, about 5.4% in 2008, a spike to about 22.7% in early 2020, dipping to about 21.2% in 2020 to 2021, about 21.2% in 2022, and ending about 23.4% in 2025. All retail (light grey #A8A8A8 line): about 0.8% in 2000, about 3.6% in 2008, a spike to about 16.4% in early 2020, about 14.7% in 2020 to 2021, about 14.2% in 2022, and ending about 16.3% in 2025. Legend at top-left of the plot area with a short coloured line swatch before each label, listed top to bottom: Core retail (red), Addressable retail (dark grey), All retail (light grey). No highlighted points beyond the 2020 spike peaks.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F1F1F1 with a white #FFFFFF band from about y 20.5% to 86.1%. Minimal, editorial, two-tone grey palette with a single red accent for the Core retail series. Title in a high-contrast modern serif (MessinaModern-Regular, close to a Modern serif such as Didone-style text serif) at roughly 5.6% of slide height, regular weight, colour #545454. Subtitle in the same serif at about 2.6% of slide height, #545454. Chart title and all chart and footer text in a geometric grotesque sans-serif (MessinaSans family): chart title semibold about 2.2%, axis labels and legend regular about 1.85%, footer author bold #A6A6A6 with date and page number in book weight #A6A6A6. Body and source text regular about 1.85% in #545454. Accent colour red #E8473B used only for the Core retail line. Lines are flat with no shadows, no gradients, no corner radii; gridlines are fine dotted #BDBDBD.

80

**Layout**

Three horizontal bands: a light grey header band (top 0 to about 20.5% of height) holding the title and subtitle, a white body band (about 20.5% to 86% of height) holding a full-width line chart, and a light grey footer band below it. The title and subtitle are left-aligned at about x 20.5%, the chart title sits at the same left edge, and the chart legend sits in the left margin at about x 4.8%. The source line sits left at x 20.5% in the footer, and the author, date and page number are right-aligned in the footer ending at about x 95.5%. The right margin is about 4.5% and the left content margin about 20.5%.

**Visuals**

Chart: line chart with markers, titled ‘Monthly ‘robotaxi’ trips in California’. Type: line with circular markers. Y axis: number of trips per month, range 0 to 1,250,000, labelled ticks at 0, 250,000, 500,000, 750,000, 1,000,000 and 1,250,000, with dotted light grey horizontal gridlines at each tick. X axis: months from about Aug 2023 to Sep 2025, labelled ticks at Sep 2023, Dec 2023, Mar 2024, Jun 2024, Sep 2024, Dec 2024, Mar 2025, June 2025 and Sep 2025, one marker per month. Series Waymo (red #EE4035 line and markers, about values read from the chart since no values are printed): Aug 2023 about 10,000; Sep 2023 about 40,000; Oct 2023 about 58,000; Nov 2023 about 58,000; Dec 2023 about 72,000; Jan 2024 about 78,000; Feb 2024 about 74,000; Mar 2024 about 84,000; Apr 2024 about 92,000; May 2024 about 145,000; Jun 2024 about 190,000; Jul 2024 about 252,000; Aug 2024 about 313,000; Sep 2024 about 355,000; Oct 2024 about 455,000; Nov 2024 about 505,000; Dec 2024 about 543,000; Jan 2025 about 552,000; Feb 2025 about 562,000; Mar 2025 about 708,000; Apr 2025 about 775,000; May 2025 about 877,000; June 2025 about 583,000; Jul 2025 about 776,000; Aug 2025 about 882,000; Sep 2025 about 1,018,000 (highest point, at the right end). Series Cruise (dark grey #545454 line and markers): about 5,000 in Aug 2023, about 10,000 in Sep 2023, about 10,000 in Oct 2023, and about 0 in Nov 2023, after which the line ends. Series Tesla: no data drawn. Legend: vertical list at top left of chart with a red marker and line for Waymo, a dark grey marker and line for Cruise, and a light grey marker and line for Tesla, each with a label. Highlighted points: none beyond the Waymo dip at June 2025. A thin grey horizontal baseline sits at 0 across the plot. Chart area is flat, with no shading or fills.

**Design**

Background light grey #F1F1F1 for the header and footer bands with a white #FFFFFF central band. Title in a serif face resembling Messina Modern (Regular, with italic for the word other), colour grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height for capitals. Subtitle in the same serif, regular weight, #545454, about 2.6% of slide height. Chart title and axis labels in a sans-serif resembling Messina Sans (SemiBold for chart title, Regular for labels), #545454 and #535353, about 1.9% to 2.2% of slide height. Source line in sans-serif Regular #545454 about 1.85%. Footer name in sans Bold #A6A6A6, date and page number in sans Book #A6A6A6, about 1.85%. Waymo series red #EE4035, Cruise series dark grey #545454, Tesla series light grey. Lines about 3 px thick with round markers about 14 px diameter. Gridlines are dotted, thin, light grey. No shadows, gradients, or rounded shapes beyond the markers.

### Slide 81

**Text**

Benedict Evans –– November 2025 81 ROBOSTORE

**Layout**

Two equal halves: a full-bleed photograph fills the left 54.9% of the slide, and a white panel occupies the right 50% with a robot photograph floating in its upper-middle area. No title. The footer sits on a single baseline at about 91% of the slide height, right-aligned in the right panel, with the author name, dash, date and page number in a row; the page number is at the right margin, about 94.5% from the left.

**Visuals**

Left: a photograph of smart glasses in close-up, cropped and full-bleed to the left edge and top and bottom edges. The black glasses frame has a glossy bronze-brown highlight along its top bar and a black temple with a small camera at the hinge. The lens displays a video call overlay: a small blue-grey thumbnail at left, a larger rounded tile at centre-right showing a smiling woman with shoulder-length brown hair in a yellow top, a faint caption on the tile's lower edge, and a grey pill-shaped button labelled End call with a handset icon beneath. Lighting is soft and even; the background is a blue-grey gradient. Right: a full-colour photograph of a humanoid robot standing front-on in a relaxed pose, with a silver and white body, black hands, black head with a cyan-lit visor, and the word ROBOSTORE printed on the chest with a small triangular logo above it. The robot is isolated on pure white, unboxed, with no shadow visible beyond a faint grounding shadow at the feet.

**Design**

Background: Light grey #F2F2F2 across the slide, with a white #FFFFFF right half behind the robot photograph. Clean, minimal two-panel layout. Background #F2F2F2 with the right panel in #FFFFFF. Footer text in MessinaSans, grey #A6A6A6: author name in bold, the dash and date and page number in book weight, about 1.85% of slide height. No titles, no accent colours beyond the photographs. Photo on left has no border or corner radius; robot photo has no frame. No shadows or gradients on the page itself apart from the photograph's own tones.

### Slide 82

**Text**

How many times have we / been here before?
We’ve had radical change (and bubbles) before
And we’ve also done automation before
Source: IBM
Benedict Evans –– November 2025 82

**Layout**

Two-panel split: a light grey left panel (about 50% width) holds the title, two body lines and a source line, left-aligned at about 4.6% x margin; a white right panel holds a single portrait advertisement image centred in it, with footer text at the bottom right of the right panel. Title sits in the vertical middle of the left panel, body lines below it; source line bottom-left and footer/page number bottom-right.

**Visuals**

One photograph/illustration: a vintage IBM advertisement (portrait orientation, 709 by 1024 px) boxed with a soft drop shadow, not cropped. Subject: a smiling-faced, serious-looking man in a white shirt and teal tie holds a large white-and-grey slide rule diagonally across his chest, with a sleeve-rolled hand gripping it; behind him a dense field of rows of identical small illustrated male heads each holding a slide rule. Composition: subject in upper-middle, heads filling the upper-left and right background, bottom third holds text. Lighting: flat, even illustration lighting. Dominant colours: cream-white background, warm flesh tones, grey and teal. Headline printed in the ad: '150 Extra Engineers' in bold script with 'Extra' underlined in red. Body text in small black serif: 'An IBM Electronic Calculator speeds through thousands of intricate computations so quickly that on many complex problems it's just like having 150 EXTRA Engineers. No longer must valuable engineering personnel ... now in critical shortage ... spend priceless creative time at routine repetitive figuring. Thousands of IBM Electronic Business Machines ... vital to our nation's defense ... are at work for science, industry, and the armed forces, in laboratories, factories, and offices, helping to meet urgent demands for greater production.' Bottom left of the ad: an illustrated man in a brown suit beside a grey mainframe computer cabinet. Bottom centre: a gold atom-orbit emblem. Bottom right: IBM logo in a gold-outlined box with 'INTERNATIONAL BUSINESS MACHINES' beside it.

**Design**

Background: Split background: left #F2F2F2 light grey, right #FFFFFF white. Left panel light grey #F2F2F2, right panel white #FFFFFF. Title in MessinaModern-Regular serif, colour #545454, about 5.56% of slide height per line, two lines. Body in MessinaModern-Book serif, colour #535353, about 3.33% of slide height, with generous line spacing between the two lines. Source line and footer in MessinaSans-Book sans-serif about 1.85% of slide height; author name in MessinaSans-Bold, the rest of footer in MessinaSans-Book, colour #A6A6A6. Photo has a soft grey drop shadow and square corners, no border. No accent colours on the slide apart from the ad's own palette.

### Slide 84

**Text**

“Automation”
Source: US Congress report on automation, 1956
In the course of the hearings, the subcommittee considered specifi-
cally six different industrial situations in the metalworking, chemical,
electronics, transportation, and communications industries, together
with data processing and officework. These industries were selected
merely as illustrative of the kind of problem which may be faced in
the trend toward automation. There are, of course, many other indus-
tries which might have been studied with interest and profit had time
permitted. The fact that these particular industries were chosen
should not for a moment obscure the fact of rapidly advancing tech-
nology in other areas. To mention only a few such areas, one might
cite the canning and bottling industries. One might cite also petro-
leum refining, the processing of commercial-bank paperwork, the
basic steel industry, the use of ready-mixed concrete, coal mining, the
use of electronically controlled elevators in our modern skyscrapers,
and numerous others.
Benedict Evans
–– November 2025
84

**Layout**

Two equal columns: a grey left half (0 to 50% width) holding the title at upper-left (top at about 25.6% height, left margin 4.6%) and the source line at bottom-left (top about 90.9%); a white right half (50 to 100% width) holding a scanned page image placed at x 52.5%, y 25.1%, spanning about 45% width, with the footer line at bottom-right (top about 91.1%) ending near 95.5% width, where the page number sits. Everything is left-aligned within its box and the content sits in the vertical middle band except for the footer line.

**Visuals**

One photograph of a scanned printed page (black-and-white, from a 1956 US Congress report) placed in a box at x 52.5%, y 25.1%, w 44.9%, h 39.7% (838 by 416 pixels). Subject: a dense block of serif body text in justified paragraphs, showing the sentence beginning 'In the course of the hearings' and ending 'and numerous others.' with a partly cut-off line below. The phrase 'electronically controlled elevators' is highlighted with a light blue (about #A9D2F5) rectangle behind the words, running from the 'use of' line across the 'elevators' word. Composition is a straight-on, tightly framed crop with the page edges visible and a faint soft drop shadow around the image. Lighting is flat and neutral; dominant colours are black text on off-white paper with the blue highlight. The image is boxed, not full-bleed, and is not overlaid except for the highlight.

**Design**

Background: Split background: left half solid light grey #F2F2F2, right half solid white #FFFFFF. Two-tone flat layout: left panel light grey #F2F2F2, right panel white #FFFFFF. Title 'Automation' in MessinaModern-Regular, a transitional serif, regular weight, dark grey #545454, about 5.6% of slide height (cap height about 3.9%). Body and footer text in MessinaSans-Book, a neutral grotesque sans-serif, regular weight, #545454 for the source line and #A6A6A6 for footer; 'Benedict Evans' in MessinaSans-Bold, #A6A6A6, about 1.85% of slide height. Accent: light blue highlight #A9D2F5 inside the image. No rules, shapes with rounded corners, or gradients; the only shadow is the soft drop shadow under the image.

### Slide 85

**Text**

“Automation” / Source: Benedict Evans / Benedict Evans / –– November 2025 / 85 / STOP / RELEASE / LIGHT / FAN / SAFETY / NYC

**Layout**

Two-column split at 50 percent. The left half is a flat light grey field holding the quotation title at upper-left (about 26 percent from the top) and a source line near the bottom-left, both left-aligned on a margin of about 4.6 percent. The right half is a full-height, full-bleed photograph. The footer row at about 91 percent height runs across the left half and the photograph's bottom edge: author name in bold, date with two en dashes, and slide number. There is no visible header or logo.

**Visuals**

One photograph, full-bleed in the right half (x 50 to 100, y 0 to 100, 1512 by 2016 px portrait, cropped to fill the half). Subject: the interior of an old wood-panelled elevator car, shot close and slightly angled. A red round STOP push-button sits in a small wooden box at the top centre. Below it a large brass control plate in a thin brass frame holds a brass lever handle with a black knob, pointing diagonally to the upper right. Engraved lettering on the plate reads RELEASE at centre, and LIGHT, FAN and SAFETY along the bottom, each with a small rectangular brass toggle slot. Two small brass screws flank the lever. Below the plate a smaller brass-framed card holds a printed NYC inspection certificate, with the NYC mark visible at its top-left corner and small print that cannot be read. At the left edge of the frame is a dark iron scissor-gate grille. Lighting is warm and directional from the upper left, with soft highlights on the brass and deep shadows in the wood grain. Dominant colours are mahogany red-brown (#7A2E1B to #B8502E), brass (#A9803F), and near-black (#1E1410). The image is not tinted or overlaid and has no border.

**Design**

Background: Solid light grey #F2F2F2 on the left half; photograph on the right half. Flat, minimal, editorial look. Background is solid light grey #F2F2F2 on the left half, with the photograph filling the right half. The title is in a high-contrast serif (Messina Modern Regular or a close Didone-style serif) at about 5.6 percent of slide height, colour #545454. Secondary text is a humanist sans (Messina Sans Book) at about 1.85 percent of slide height. Source line is #545454; the footer name is Messina Sans Bold #A6A6A6, and the date and page number are Messina Sans Book #A6A6A6. Shapes: none. The photograph has square corners, no shadow, and no gradient.

- photo at x 50, y 0, w 50, h 100 [#7A2E1B]: Close-up photograph of an old wood-panelled elevator car control panel with a brass lever, STOP button, RELEASE, LIGHT, FAN and SAFETY labels, and an NYC inspection certificate (Full-bleed, cropped to fill the right half, square corners, no border, no overlay or tint. Warm directional light from upper left.)

Line chart titled 'Elevator attendants, USA'. Type: smooth single-series line chart, no markers. Y-axis: 0 to 100,000, gridlines at 0, 25,000, 50,000, 75,000 and 100,000 (dotted light grey horizontal lines); axis labels are plain numbers with thousands separators. X-axis: years 1900 to 1990 labelled every 10 years, baseline solid grey line. Series values (estimated, no values printed): about 13,000 in 1900, about 25,000 in 1910, about 41,000 in 1920, about 67,000 in 1930, about 84,000 in 1940, about 94,000 in 1950 (peak), about 77,000 in 1960, about 40,000 in 1970, about 22,000 in 1980, about 11,000 in 1990.
